managed by `scripts/dev.ts` (spawns it, `.omniharness/bridge.lock.json` lockfile, `src/server/dev/managed-bridge.ts`, `src/server/dev/bridge-lock.ts`)\n\n### Process B: the Next.js server — holds the rest\nStarted by `src/instrumentation.ts` (`register()`, nodejs runtime only):\n1. `await dbReady` (SQLite schema init)\n2. `ensureSupervisorRuntimeStarted()` → `src/server/supervisor/runtime-watchdog.ts`: **`setInterval` every 15s** doing `syncRunningSupervision()`, wake rehydration, output compaction, stuck-worker reaping, bridge session sync\n3. `ensureClaudeModelGatewayStartedAtBoot()` + shutdown handlers → `src/server/integrations/claude-model-gateway/process-manager.ts` **spawns a `cliproxyapi` child process**\n\nOther in-process singletons and child-process owners inside the Next server:\n- **`src/server/terminal/terminal-manager.ts`** — `node-pty` PTYs. Singleton stashed on `process.__omniHarnessTerminalManager` (line 349) to survive HMR. Holds per-session ring buffers (256 KB), subscriber sets, idle reaper (`setInterval` 10s), 30-min idle timeout. Registers `process.once('exit'|'SIGTERM'|'SIGINT')` handlers.\n- **`src/server/events/named-events.ts`** — module-level **500-entry ring buffer** (`const ring: BufferedEntry[]`, `let cursor = 0`, line 547-549). This is the SSE replay backbone.\n- **`src/server/events/live-updates.ts`** — module-level `Set<listener>` + `notificationVersion` counter. The SSE loop parks on `waitForEventStreamNotification()`; mutation paths call `notifyEventStreamSubscribers()`. **Purely same-process.**\n- **`src/server/db/index.ts:954-958`** — DB client map on `process.__omniHarnessDbStates`\n- **`src/server/auth/guards.ts:14-19`** — API session cache on `process.__omniHarnessApiSessionCache` (10s TTL, 128 entries)\n- **`src/server/session-providers/process-store.ts:35`** — `const liveProcesses = new Map<string, ProcessHandle>()` for spawned \"process sessions\"\n- `src/server/supervisor/index.ts` uses `spawnSync`; `src/server/git/command.ts`, `src/server/git/auto-commit.ts`, `src/server/external-sessions/running.ts`, `src/server/accounts/migration.ts`, `src/server/restart-control.ts`, `src/server/process-ownership.ts` all shell out.\n\n---\n\n## 4. Persistence\n\n**SQLite** via `@libsql/client` + `drizzle-orm` (also `better-sqlite3` present).\n- Path: `src/server/db/index.ts:6` → `getAppDataPath('sqlite.db')`; root from `OMNIHARNESS_ROOT` or `process.cwd()` (`src/server/app-root.ts`). Override with `OMNIHARNESS_DB_PATH`.\n- Live DB in repo root is **~95 MB** + a 4 MB WAL.\n- Schema: `src/server/db/schema.ts` — 30 tables: `plans`, `runs`, `workers`, `process_sessions`, `worker_counters`, `messages`, `conversation_read_markers`, `queued_conversation_messages`, `recovery_incidents`, `supervisor_scheduled_wakes`, `accounts`, `account_secrets`, `worker_credential_allocations`, `worker_token_usage`, `account_usage_snapshots`, `credit_events`, `settings`, `auth_sessions`, `notification_subscriptions`, `auth_pair_tokens`, `auth_events`, `plan_items`, `clarifications`, `worker_assignments`, `execution_events`, `supervisor_interventions`, `planning_review_runs`, `planning_review_rounds`, `planning_review_findings`, `artifact_streams`.\n- `drizzle.config.ts` at repo root.\n\n**File-based storage** (this is the part that makes \"remote runner\" nontrivial):\n- **Worker conversation streams**: append-only JSONL, one per worker, at `<projectPath>/.omniharness/run-data/<runId>/<workerId>.jsonl` (`src/server/workers/output-store.ts`, `src/server/artifacts/project-root.ts`). Falls back to `<appData>/run-data/<runId>`. SQLite stores only the relative path.\n- Attachments: `getAppDataPath(\"attachments\")` (repo `attachments/`, 25 entries)\n- Per-project scoped CLI homes: `<cwd>/.omniharness/cli-home/<agent>/`\n- Per-account CLI homes: `getAppDataPath(\"account-cli-homes/<cliType>/<accountId>\")`\n- Credential profiles: `<root>/.omniharness/credential-profiles`\n- Auth key: `getAppDataPath(\".omniharness-auth.key\")`; settings encryption key: `~/.omniharness/settings.key` (`src/server/settings/crypto.ts`)\n- Claude gateway managed files: `~/.omniharness/cliproxyapi`\n- Diagnostics: `.omniharness/memory-trace.jsonl`, `.omniharness/bridge-validation-failures.log`, `.omniharness/*.log`, `.omniharness/bridge.lock.json`\n- Project memory: `<projectPath>/.omniharness/memory/`\n- `config/accounts.yml`, `shared/locales/*.json`\n\n**Settings** live in the `settings` SQLite table, encrypted with a local key file. VAPID web-push keypair is generated once and stored there too (`src/server/notifications/web-push.ts`).\n\n---\n\n## 5. Real-time streams\n\n### Terminal output — two *different* things named \"terminal\"\n\n1. **`src/components/Terminal.tsx` (2,553 lines) is NOT a PTY.** It is the agent-conversation renderer. It takes a `entries: WorkerEntry[]` prop and renders markdown/tool-calls/thoughts. Data arrives via:\n   - `src/app/home/WorkerEntriesManager.ts` (830 lines) — paginated `GET /api/workers/:workerId/entries?afterSeq=|beforeSeq=|limit=`\n   - plus the `/api/events` SSE `update` snapshots\n   - It imports `WorkerEntry` from `@/server/workers/entries-types` and helpers from `@/lib/agent-output`.\n\n2. **`src/components/InteractiveTerminal.tsx` (160 lines) IS the PTY**, using `@xterm/xterm` + `@xterm/addon-fit`:\n   - `POST /api/terminals` → `{terminalId, cols, rows}`\n   - `new EventSource('/api/terminals/:id/stream')` for output\n   - `POST /api/terminals/:id/input` for stdin, `/resize`, `DELETE` to kill\n\n### Event stream mechanics (`src/runtime/http/routes/events.ts`)\nA hybrid **push + long-poll-with-parking** loop:\n- `src/app/home/LiveEventConnectionManager.ts` (449 lines) owns the `EventSource`, reconnect, and `Last-Event-ID` bookkeeping\n- `src/app/home/EventStreamStateManager.ts` + `EventStreamSnapshotCacheManager.ts` reduce frames into client state\n- Server loop: drain ring buffer → build full snapshot payload → race against `RUNTIME_AGENT_GRACE_MS` (falls back to persisted-only payload, then upgrades) → emit `update` if the serialized payload changed → park on `waitForEventStreamNotification(STREAM_REFRESH_INTERVAL_MS, version)` → heartbeat on timeout\n- Snapshot markers are allocated from the same ring so SSE ids stay monotonic across `update` frames and named events\n\n**No polling fallback other than the snapshot refetch; no WebSockets anywhere.**\n\n---\n\n## 6. Shared client/server code → future shared package\n\nTruly isomorphic today (`src/lib/`, 4,935 LOC, zero Node builtins, zero `@/server` runtime imports):\n- `agent-output.ts` (1,460) — **the big one**; type-only import of `WorkerEntryChannel|WorkerEntryType` from `@/server/workers/entries-types`\n- `worker-terminal-processes.ts`, `claude-session-model.ts`, `claude-model-gateway.ts`, `chat-attachments.ts`, `conversation-workers.ts`, `acp/elicitation-schema.ts`, `app-errors.ts`, `run-recovery-state.ts`, `conversations.ts`, `worker-terminal-messages.ts`, `project-file-links.ts`, `syntax-highlighting.ts`, `project-paths.ts`, `i18n-core.ts`/`i18n.ts`, `git-workspace.ts`, `runtime-resource-settings.ts`, `superseded-entries.ts`, `worker-stuck-events.ts`, `use-manager-snapshot.ts`, `conversation-state.ts`, `state-manager.ts`, `pwa.ts`, `local-pairing-token.ts`, `project-scope.ts`, `commit-workflow.ts`, `mentions.ts`, `run-status.ts`, `conversation-visuals.ts`, `plan-path.ts`, `planning-output.ts`, `utils.ts`, `auth-constants.ts`\n\nType contracts that **live in `src/server/` but are imported by client components** — these must move into the shared package:\n| File | Imported by |\n|---|---|\n| `src/server/workers/entries-types.ts` | `components/Terminal.tsx:19`, `components/WorkerCard.tsx:16`, `lib/agent-output.ts:1` |\n| `src/server/planning/review-preferences.ts` | `components/PlanningReviewControls.tsx:12`, `components/PlanningArtifactsPanel.tsx:14`, `app/home/useHomeMutations.ts:19` |\n| `src/server/events/named-events.ts` | (types) `src/runtime/http/registry.ts`, `src/runtime/http/context.ts`, `src/runtime/index.ts` |\n\nAlso shared-by-nature:\n- `src/app/home/types.ts` (435 lines) — `EventStreamState`, `SettingsResponse`, `AuthSessionResponse`; already re-exported through `src/runtime/bootstrap.ts`\n- `src/runtime-api/types.ts` — the `RuntimeAPIs` contract\n- `shared/locales/*.json` — 9 locale files, already outside `src/`\n\n**Caveat:** `src/server/workers/entries-types.ts` is a pure-types file today (only import is the file header comment referencing bridge-client), but `src/server/planning/review-preferences.ts` imports `zod` — fine for a shared package.\n\n---\n\n## 7. Existing separation hints (there are many)\n\n| Hint | Location |\n|---|---|\n| **Design doc for exactly this** | `docs/architecture/common-runtime-multi-surface.md` — 237 lines, route-by-route migration table, all rows \"Migrated\" |\n| **Portable route registry** | `src/runtime/http/registry.ts` — Fetch-`Request`/`Response`, own dynamic `:param` matcher, framework-free |\n| **Standalone Node HTTP server** | `src/runtime/http/server.ts` — `startOmniHttpServer()` / `startOmniServer()`, optional `staticDir` for serving a built SPA |\n| **Runtime lifecycle handle** | `src/runtime/index.ts` — `createOmniRuntime({surface, label, hooks})`, emits `runtime.started`/`stopped`/`start_failed` |\n| **Non-Next SPA entry** | `src/ui/render-web.tsx` — `createRoot(...).render(<OmniApp bootstrap runtimeApis/>)`, no Next |\n| **`/api/runtime/bootstrap`** | HTTP equivalent of the SSR bootstrap, so a non-Next shell can hydrate |\n| **Electron shell** | `apps/electron/main.ts`, `apps/electron/src/runtime.ts`, `preload.ts`, `src/native-bridge.ts`, `scripts/build.mjs` |\n| **VSCode shell** | `apps/vscode/`, `src/vscode-extension/bridge.ts`, `src/runtime-api/vscode.ts` — already talks to a **remote** `serverUrl` |\n| **Base-URL support in the web client** | `src/runtime-api/web.ts` — `WebRuntimeApiOptions.baseUrl` |\n| **Env vars for remote endpoints** | `OMNI_SERVER_URL`, `OMNI_ELECTRON_RENDERER_URL`, `OMNIHARNESS_BRIDGE_URL`, `OMNIHARNESS_PUBLIC_ORIGIN`, `OMNIHARNESS_AGENT_RUNTIME_HOST/PORT`, `OMNIHARNESS_WEB_HOST`, `OMNIHARNESS_ROOT`, `OMNIHARNESS_DB_PATH`, `OMNIHARNESS_RUNTIME_DATA_DIR`, `OMNIHARNESS_APPDATA_DIR` |\n| Multi-process orchestrator | `scripts/dev.ts` (web + bridge + compression proxy), `scripts/start.ts`, `controller.sh`, `omni`, `omni.sh`, `start.sh` |\n| Tests already scoped by surface | `tests/runtime/`, `tests/runtime-api/`, `tests/electron/`, `tests/vscode/`, `tests/lifecycle/` (drives control plane over HTTP/SSE, no browser) |\n\n**What is NOT there:** no `server.js` custom Next server, no `output: \"standalone\"` in `next.config.ts`, no `middleware.ts`, no `NEXT_PUBLIC_*` API-base env var.\n\n`next.config.ts` contents: `allowedDevOrigins` (3 tunnel hostnames), `htmlLimitedBots: /.*/ `, one rewrite `/session/:runId → /?run=:runId`, and turbopack/webpack aliasing of `encoding` to `src/shims/empty-encoding.ts`.\n\n---\n\n## 8. Auth model\n\nCookie session + optional password + QR pairing. All server-side, no third-party IdP.\n\n- Cookie `omni_session` (`src/server/auth/config.ts:6`), `HttpOnly; SameSite=Lax; Path=/`, `Secure` in production — set in `src/runtime/http/routes/cookies.ts`\n- Opaque token (id.secret) hashed into `auth_sessions` table; idle 30d / absolute 90d\n- Methods: `password_login` | `qr_pair` (`src/server/auth/session.ts:14`)\n- Password: [REDACTED] or `OMNIHARNESS_AUTH_PASSWORD_HASH` (argon2 via `@node-rs/argon2`); key file `.omniharness-auth.key`\n- Pairing: `src/server/auth/pairing.ts` + `auth_pair_tokens`; QR flow in `src/components/PairDeviceDialog.tsx`, `?pair=<token>` URL param handled in `src/runtime/bootstrap.ts`\n- Enforcement: `requireApiSession(...)` in `src/server/auth/guards.ts`, called in ~37 places across `src/runtime/http/routes/*` with `enforceSameOrigin: true` on all mutations\n- Rate limiting: `src/server/auth/rate-limit.ts`; audit trail: `src/server/auth/audit.ts` + `auth_events` table\n- Bypasses: `OMNIHARNESS_E2E_BYPASS_AUTH`, `OMNIHARNESS_TEST_BYPASS_AUTH`, `isAutomationAuthBypassEnabled()`\n- **No CORS headers anywhere.** `grep -i \"access-control-allow\"` over `src/` returns nothing.\n\n---\n\n## 9. Next-specific server features vs plain React\n\n**Very little Next-specific server surface.** Effectively three things:\n\n1. **One server component tree**: `src/app/layout.tsx` (+ `next/font/google` Geist) and `src/app/page.tsx`. `page.tsx` is 11 lines: it awaits `buildHomeBootstrap(searchParams)` and renders `<OmniApp bootstrap/>`. `src/app/home/bootstrap.server.ts` is the only user of `next/headers`, and it calls `buildRuntimeBootstrap({ includeInitialData: false })` — so **SSR currently fetches almost nothing**; the client re-bootstraps.\n2. **`src/instrumentation.ts`** — the boot hook for the supervisor watchdog + gateway.\n3. **`next/server`'s `NextRequest`** — used by `src/server/auth/guards.ts`, `src/server/auth/session.ts`, `src/server/api-errors.ts`. The portable routes bridge with a 9-line shim: `src/runtime/http/routes/next-request.ts` (`new NextRequest(url, {method, headers, signal})`).\n\nEverything else is plain client React: 61 files with `\"use client\"`; `HomeApp` is loaded via `next/dynamic({ ssr: false })` behind a `<BootShell/>`. State is TanStack Query + hand-rolled Manager singletons (`src/lib/state-manager.ts` + `useManagerSnapshot`); **zustand is in package.json but unused**.\n\n`next/image` is used in 5 components (`Terminal.tsx`, `cli-brand-icons.tsx`, `AttachmentImagePreviewDialog.tsx`, `home/UserInputMessage.tsx`, `home/ConversationComposer.tsx`) and `next/dynamic` in 4 — all trivially replaceable.\n\nNon-server-component `.tsx` files under `src/app`/`src/components` that lack `\"use client\"` are mostly leaf presentational components pulled into client trees (all of `src/components/ui/*`, `src/components/settings/*`, most of `src/components/home/*`) — they're not real RSCs.\n\n---\n\n## 10. Load-bearing issues for the split\n\nOrdered by how much they'll hurt.\n\n**A. The event bus is same-process memory.** `src/server/events/named-events.ts` (500-entry ring, module-level) and `src/server/events/live-updates.ts` (module-level listener `Set` + version counter) are the entire real-time backbone. `emitNamedEvent` is called from ~everywhere in `src/server/`. If the runner is one process this is fine — but it means **the runner must be a single process**: you cannot horizontally scale it, and the Next server cannot be a separate process from the supervisor without losing every SSE frame. Today the Next server *is* the event bus owner; the bridge (7800) is separate and its state is polled into snapshots rather than pushed.\n\n**B. `src/runtime/http/server.ts` cannot stream.** `writeFetchResponse` (lines 72–79) does `Buffer.from(await fetchResponse.arrayBuffer())` before `response.end()`. For `/api/events` and `/api/terminals/:id/stream` — which return never-ending `ReadableStream`s — this buffers forever. The standalone runner server therefore **does not currently support either SSE endpoint**, even though both are registered in the registry. This must be fixed (pipe the web stream to the `ServerResponse`, flush headers early, disable Nagle) before the runner can be the real server.\n\n**C. Auth is cookie + same-origin; a remote frontend is cross-origin.** `enforceSameOrigin: true` on every mutation, `SameSite=Lax` cookies, and zero CORS headers mean an Electron/Tauri/mobile app on a different origin cannot authenticate today. The VSCode extension sidesteps this by proxying through the extension host with an explicit `sessionCookie` (`src/vscode-extension/bridge.ts`). For multi-runner you'll want a bearer-token scheme (the pairing tokens in `auth_pair_tokens` are the natural seed) plus explicit CORS + per-runner credential storage on the client.\n\n**D. Multi-runner is not modeled anywhere.** `RuntimeAPIs` (`src/runtime-api/types.ts`) is a flat singleton with no runner identity. `web.ts` has a single `baseUrl`. The bootstrap payload, all the Manager singletons in `src/app/home/`, the SSE cursor (`initialLastEventId`), and the snapshot checksum cache are all implicitly scoped to \"the one server\". Adding N runners means: runner id on every entity key, one `EventSource` + one `lastEventId` cursor per runner, per-runner query cache keys, and per-runner auth.\n\n**E. Filesystem-coupled APIs assume the client can see the runner's disk.** `/api/fs`, `/api/fs/files`, `/api/attachments`, `/api/projects/memory`, `/api/git`, project paths in conversation creation, and the JSONL worker streams under `<projectPath>/.omniharness/run-data/` are all runner-local paths surfaced raw to the UI (`FolderPickerDialog.tsx`, `FileAttachmentPickerDialog.tsx`, `FileViewerPanel.tsx`, `ComposerContainer.tsx`). Electron's `native.chooseFolder()` returns a *local* path, which is wrong once the runner is remote. This needs an explicit \"paths belong to the runner, not the client\" model.\n\n**F. Process-object singletons.** `process.__omniHarnessTerminalManager`, `process.__omniHarnessDbStates`, `process.__omniHarnessApiSessionCache`, `liveProcesses` in `session-providers/process-store.ts`, plus `AgentRuntimeManager`'s maps. All fine for a single runner process; all break under any multi-process runner. Also note `getTerminalManager()` registers `process.once('exit'|'SIGTERM'|'SIGINT')` — a runner needs one owner of shutdown, not per-module handlers.\n\n**G. `src/server/` imports leak into client components.** Only 5 files (§6), all type-only except `review-preferences.ts` (which exports a zod schema — a real runtime import in `PlanningReviewControls.tsx` and `PlanningArtifactsPanel.tsx`). Small but must be resolved before the frontend can build without `src/server/` on disk.\n\n**H. The 29 direct-`fetch` call sites** (§2d) are the actual bulk of the work. Until `useHomeMutations.ts` (1,269 lines), `useHomeQueries.ts`, `LiveEventConnectionManager.ts`, `WorkerEntriesManager.ts`, `GitWorkspaceManager.ts`, `ProjectMemoryPanelManager.ts`, `ConversationNotificationManager.ts` and the dialog components route through `RuntimeAPIs`, hardcoded `/api/...` relative URLs pin the frontend to same-origin.\n\n**I. Web push binds notifications to a browser origin.** VAPID keys live in the runner's `settings` table; `notification_subscriptions` are browser push endpoints; `public/sw.js` is a service worker. A native/mobile shell needs a different notification path (the `RuntimeAPIs.native.notify` hook exists for Electron already).\n\n**J. Two servers must be co-located today.** The bridge (7800) and the Next server share the filesystem (JSONL run-data under project paths, CLI homes, credential profiles, `.omniharness/` lockfiles) and `process.cwd()` via `getAppRoot()`. They are one logical runner, not two independently deployable things.\n\n**K. Dev-only remote-control surface.** `src/server/restart-control.ts` + `scripts/remote-restart.ts` + `OMNIHARNESS_REMOTE_RESTART_TOKEN`/`_PASSWORD`/`_HOST`/`_PORT` implement an out-of-band restart channel writing to `.omniharness/remote-restart.pid.json`. Worth deciding whether that becomes a runner-management API or gets dropped.\n\n---\n\n## Suggested target shape (given what exists)\n\n```\npackages/shared        <- src/lib/* + entries-types.ts + review-preferences.ts\n                          + app/home/types.ts + runtime-api/types.ts + shared/locales\npackages/runner        <- src/server/* + src/runtime/* + instrumentation logic\n                          entry = fixed src/runtime/http/server.ts (streaming!)\n                          absorbs scripts/agent-runtime.ts as an internal subsystem\npackages/interface     <- src/ui + src/components + src/app/home + src/runtime-api\n                          Vite SPA; src/ui/render-web.tsx is already the entry\napps/web (optional)    <- thin Next wrapper, or delete Next entirely\napps/electron          <- exists; switch from in-process runtime to remote client\napps/vscode            <- exists; already remote-capable\n```\n\nThe three concrete blockers to unblock first, in order: **(B)** make the standalone server stream, **(C)** token auth + CORS, **(D)** runner identity in `RuntimeAPIs`. Everything else is mechanical."
  }
]

## Claude thinking · 2026-07-29T11:27:07.821Z

I'm recognizing this is fundamentally an architecture and infrastructure decision rather than a UI design problem, so the product review isn't the right lens here. The core question I need to explore with the user is what happens to Next.js in this multi-runner setup—whether it stays as a thin web wrapper or gets replaced entirely.

The immediate tension is that keeping Next solves the "preserve the web version" requirement but doesn't address the 8GB dev RAM issue, whereas switching to a Vite SPA served by the runner would eliminate that overhead while still maintaining the same user-facing interface. I'm leaning toward recommending the full Vite approach since it simplifies the architecture and solves the performance problem directly.

Beyond that, I need to clarify the desktop and mobile strategy—whether to stick with the existing Electron setup or explore Tauri, and whether the phone experience should be a PWA or native. There's also the question of whether the runner should consolidate the bridge and web server into a single process, and how authentication flows work for remote connections. Finally, I need to understand the scope: is the multi-runner connection manager part of this milestone, or should we start with a single remote runner first? tly says one at a time, but the tool supports batching multiple questions in a single call, which feels like a reasonable middle ground. I'll formulate the key decisions: whether to replace Next with Vite for the web layer, which native shell to use (Electron, Tauri, or PWA), and how to handle mobile delivery — leaning toward keeping Electron for desktop while using PWA for phone access initially, with Tauri as a future option if needed.

For the runner architecture, I'm thinking the bridge separation made sense when Next dev restarts were frequent, but once the UI becomes a static SPA, that isolation matters less. I could simplify to a single logical runner with the bridge as an internal child process rather than maintaining two separate processes.

On multi-runner support for v1, I'm leaning toward a switcher that lets you connect to multiple runners but view one at a time—not trying to merge views yet. That's simpler to ship and still keeps the architecture open for merged views later.

The worktree question can wait until the planning stage. I'll ask about these decisions through the user question flow.

type PageSearchParams = Record<string, string | string[] | undefined>;

export type RuntimeBootstrapOptions = {
  searchParams?: PageSearchParams;
  requestHeaders: Headers;
  includeInitialData?: boolean;
};

function firstParam(value: string | string[] | undefined) {
  return Array.isArray(value) ? value[0] ?? "" : value ?? "";
}

function normalizeParam(value: string | string[] | undefined) {
  return firstParam(value).trim() || null;
}

function buildRequestUrl(searchParams: PageSearchParams, requestHeaders: Headers) {
  const host = requestHeaders.get("x-forwarded-host")?.split(",")[0]?.trim()
    || requestHeaders.get("host")?.trim()
    || "localhost";
  const protocol = requestHeaders.get("x-forwarded-proto")?.split(",")[0]?.trim() || "http";
  const url = new URL(`${protocol}://${host}/`);

  for (const [key, rawValue] of Object.entries(searchParams)) {
    const values = Array.isArray(rawValue) ? rawValue : [rawValue];
    for (const value of values) {
      if (value != null) {
        url.searchParams.append(key, value);
      }
    }
  }

  return url;
}

export async function buildRuntimeBootstrap({
  searchParams = {},
  requestHeaders,
  includeInitialData = true,
}: RuntimeBootstrapOptions): Promise<HomeBootstrapPayload> {
  const requestUrl = buildRequestUrl(searchParams, requestHeaders);
  const selectedRunId = normalizeParam(searchParams.run);
  const draftProjectPath = normalizeParam(searchParams.project);
  const pairTokenFromUrl = normalizeParam(searchParams.pair);

  const session = await buildAuthSessionState({
    url: requestUrl.toString(),
    headers: requestHeaders,
  });
  const appUnlocked = Boolean(session && (!session.enabled || session.authenticated));

  const [settings, initialEventState] = appUnlocked && includeInitialData
    ? await Promise.all([
        readSettingsState(),
        buildPersistedEventPayload({ selectedRunId }),
      ])

## Claude thinking · 2026-07-29T11:42:58.691Z

Looking at the SSR setup, the server renders the page layout and BootShell skeleton through `page.tsx` with `<OmniApp bootstrap>`, while `HomeApp` stays client-side only via dynamic import. The bootstrap payload gathers route params and auth session state from cookies server-side, but the Next page configuration excludes the settings and initial event state from being passed along.

There's already an HTTP endpoint at `GET /api/runtime/bootstrap` that Electron and VSCode shells use, so the current "SSR" is really just a static shell plus an inlined server-computed JSON blob that saves one round-trip for auth before showing the lock screen. The actual app tree renders entirely on the client. I can replicate this without Next by serving an index.html and either fetching the bootstrap endpoint on load (which adds a round-trip but the BootShell skeleton displays while loading anyway since HomeApp is dynamically loaded), or injecting the bootstrap JSON directly into the HTML at serve time using template injection to keep it at zero round-trips.

Since the runner will serve the SPA regardless, template injection is straightforward — the standalone server already supports a static directory, so I can add a hook to inline the bootstrap payload into index.html, making the SSR argument moot.

Looking at the user's actual selections: they only checked "Keep Electron" for shells, though they mentioned wanting to install the interface on a phone and connect to remote runners. The PWA support already exists in the app today and will keep working since the runner serves the SPA, so I'll note that as an option. For multi-runner, they want a runner switcher. On topology, they chose to keep both runner and bridge network-exposed, which is interesting because the client never actually talks to the bridge directly — it's only consumed by the server.

Now for the Next.js question: they pushed back asking me to double-check since they're using SSR. I need to present my findings clearly — SSR only renders the boot shell and inlines the bootstrap payload, while HomeApp is marked as ssr:false. Given their pushback, I should present this evidence and ask them to confirm whether they still want to drop Next.js, rather than just assuming.

## Claude · 2026-07-29T11:42:59.159Z

You were right to make me check — here's exactly what SSR does today, verified line by line:

- `src/app/page.tsx:8-11` server-renders `<OmniApp bootstrap>`, **but** `OmniApp` loads the entire app via `dynamic(() => import("HomeApp"), { ssr: false })` (`src/ui/OmniApp.tsx:10-16`). So the server-rendered HTML is only the root layout (fonts, theme script, PWA metadata) plus the 31-line `BootShell` skeleton. Every real component — sidebar, conversations, terminal — is client-rendered only, even today.
- The one real SSR benefit is the **bootstrap payload**: `buildHomeBootstrap` computes auth session state + route params server-side and inlines it into the HTML, saving one round-trip before deciding lock-screen vs app. And it's called with `includeInitialData: false` (`src/runtime/bootstrap.ts`), so settings and event snapshots are *not* SSR'd — the client re-fetches those anyway.
- That exact payload is already exposed as `GET /api/runtime/bootstrap` for the Electron/VSCode shells, and the standalone server (`src/runtime/http/server.ts`) can serve a static SPA dir. The zero-round-trip property is preservable without Next: the runner injects the bootstrap JSON into `index.html` at serve time — a ~20-line template step.

So the app "uses SSR" in the sense of a server-computed boot blob + skeleton, not server-rendered UI. Given that, the trade-off stands as I described it:

## Claude thinking · 2026-07-29T11:52:17.495Z

I've settled on the key architectural decisions: moving Kill Next to a Vite SPA that the runner serves with injected bootstrap code, keeping Electron for shells since the PWA already works, adding a runner switcher for multi-runner support, and maintaining the two exposed services rather than merging them. Now I'm working through the PM pass and completeness review before proposing different approaches and documenting the full design in the spec file.

For the design itself, I need to clarify the core goal and architecture: separating the interface layer (static SPA via Vite plus Electron shell and PWA) from the runner (a headless Node server combining omni-server and the agent-runtime bridge). The runner stays as two exposed services since the bridge isn't hit by client-side code anyway, and I should verify the deployment strategy for both dev and production environments.

On the runner side, I'm promoting the existing startOmniServer function to be the production entry point, but first I need to fix the streaming issue for SSE routes by properly piping ReadableStream to ServerResponse. The boot sequence should replicate what instrumentation.ts currently handles—database readiness, supervisor watchdog, and the claude-model-gateway—using the createOmniRuntime hooks. The runner will serve the built SPA from a static directory with bootstrap injection into index.html, and I need to add bearer token auth for remote clients alongside CORS configuration, keeping the cookie flow for same-origin web UI access while supporting token-based auth for Electron and remote connections. I also need to remove the NextRequest dependency from auth guards by using the existing shim and dropping the next/server import entirely.

For the interface, the Vite SPA entry point already exists in src/ui/render-web.tsx, so I'm replacing the Next.js-specific imports—next/dynamic, next/image, and next/font—with standard alternatives, and converting layout.tsx into index.html with a React root. The RuntimeAPIs will become per-runner...

On the client side, I'm building a RunnerRegistry or ConnectionManager that holds multiple runner profiles with name, URL, and token, persisted via localStorage for the web/PWA and electron store for desktop, with an active runner context and UI switcher. The bulk of the work is migrating about 29 direct-fetch files over to use RuntimeAPIs, and I need to namespace the per-runner SSE cursors and query cache keys by runner ID.

For the repo structure, the pnpm workspace already exists, so I'm deciding between keeping src/ mostly as-is with lint rule boundaries versus splitting into actual packages like packages/shared, packages/runner, and packages/interface. The Explore report outlined a target shape, but for the first milestone I'm leaning toward incremental changes to avoid a massive refactor.

Rather than moving 460 files around, I'd keep everything in one package with two separate build targets—one for the runner (tsx/node) and one for the interface (Vite)—and enforce boundaries through ESLint rules and build-time checks. This lets the interface bundle avoid importing from src/server except for types, which I can move to a shared types location. Package extraction can happen later as a separate milestone if needed.

I'm weighing three main approaches: staying in-place with dual builds (least disruptive), extracting into a full monorepo structure (cleaner long-term but massive churn now), or keeping Next as the frontend host (already ruled out). The roadmap breaks down into four milestones—first getting the runner standalone with proper streaming and auth, then migrating the interface to Vite, then handling the RuntimeAPIs migration and multi-runner support, and finally building out the Electron client.

The sequencing matters because token auth and fetch migration unlock remote capability, while Vite solves the RAM pressure that's been a pain point. I need to cover the data flow architecture, error handling, how runner profiles and settings persist, the per-runner state model, event naming conventions, and the control plane (CLI already exists). For testing, I'm building on the existing lifecycle tests but adding runner boot validation, SSE streaming verification, cross-origin auth checks, and multi-runner client state scenarios. The main risks are around token security when the runner sits on the network and getting CORS policies right.

On the file side, manager.ts and Terminal.tsx are too large but don't need refactoring for this work—useHomeMutations is the one that'll get heavily touched by the RuntimeAPIs migration, so I'll split that as part of the migration effort. For the PM angle, the primary user is Daniel running the runner on his Mac or servers while controlling it from a laptop or phone, supervising coding agents remotely. The core workflow is managing runner profiles and pairing new ones.

The supporting features include connection status visibility, reconnect/offline handling, version mismatch detection between interface and runner, secure exposure guidance, and push notifications from remote runners using per-runner VAPID keys. The state lives split between client (runner registry, auth tokens, SSE cursors, caches) and runner (domain state). Failure modes are handled with offline banners and retries, re-auth flows for expired tokens, and warnings for version skew. First-time setup shows a "connect a runner" screen with URL and pairing, and I'm thinking through the risk surfaces around exposure and security.

For the baseline v1 UI, I'd include a runner switcher in the header, a settings panel for managing connections, and status indicators. Some additions like version-skew checking seem cheap enough to include, while others like a multi-runner dashboard can wait. I'm leaning toward presenting a condensed design summary to get approval before diving into the full spec.

The workflow I'm settling on: draft the design, run it through second-opinion for architecture validation, write the spec, then present a summary for review. This avoids excessive back-and-forth while still getting feedback on the critical pieces.

I should verify that keeping the bridge bound to localhost by default (via OMNIHARNESS_AGENT_RUNTIME_HOST) satisfies the "two exposed services" constraint — only omni-server needs remote exposure since clients never talk to bridge directly. I'll document that clearly in the design.

Before finalizing, I need to check the dev orchestration in scripts/dev.ts, confirm PWA functionality still works, and verify the attachment upload and QR pairing flows.

For the pairing mechanism with remote runners, the flow uses GET/POST /api/auth/pair to generate a token, then passes it as a ?pair=<token> URL parameter. For Electron remote connections, the pairing token gets redeemed for a session, and I can add a design detail where POST /api/auth/pair/redeem returns a bearer token instead of a cookie when needed.

Now I'll draft the spec in docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-29-interface-runner-split-design.md, starting with goal & constraints, then the decisions already made.

The spec will cover the current state, target architecture across the runner, auth & remote access, interface layer, multi-runner client model, and shared code boundaries. I'm also considering the in-place versus package extraction approach for the runner — I'll recommend in-place with two targets in the spec and flag package extraction as a future option, since this is a debatable architectural choice worth presenting for review.

I need to flesh out the state model and client/server invariants around per-runner ownership and SSE cursors, then cover persistence for runner profiles and tokens, user stories with acceptance criteria, product completeness across onboarding and offline states, instrumentation with named events for runner boot and auth flows, testing strategy including agentic journey tests, milestones, risks and trust surfaces, and open questions. I'm also noting that the filesystem-coupled UI issue with FolderPickerDialog uses the runner's /api/fs endpoint, so folder picking is tied to the runner's filesystem.

The Electron native folder picker returns local paths and needs to be disabled when connected to a remote runner, while attachment uploads through /api/attachments work fine remotely since paths belong to the runner. For notifications, web push works for PWA served from the runner with per-runner VAPID keys and browser-scoped subscriptions, and Electron has its own native notification hook. Now I'm ready to write out the spec file and then get a second opinion on it.

The existing VSCode shell keeps working (it is already remote-capable).\n\nMotivations, in priority order:\n\n1. Interface installable on a phone/laptop connecting to **multiple remote\n   runners**.\n2. Web version keeps working exactly as it does now (same URL shape, same UI).\n\n## 2. Decisions already made (user-confirmed)\n\n| Decision | Choice |\n|---|---|\n| Next.js | **Remove.** Interface becomes a Vite SPA; runner serves the built bundle and injects the bootstrap JSON into `index.html` at serve time (preserves today's zero-round-trip boot; verified that the app tree is `ssr:false` and SSR only emits the BootShell skeleton + bootstrap blob). |\n| Native shells v1 | **Electron** (existing `apps/electron`, converted to pure remote client). Phone = existing PWA support, now served by the runner. VSCode shell unchanged. Tauri: future option, out of scope. |\n| Multi-runner UI v1 | **Runner switcher**: save N runner profiles, connect, switch; one runner's data on screen at a time. Merged cross-runner views: future. |\n| Backend topology | **Keep two services** as today: the omni API server and the agent-runtime bridge (port 7800) stay separate processes. Clients only ever talk to the API server; the bridge keeps binding to localhost by default (`OMNIHARNESS_AGENT_RUNTIME_HOST`). A \"runner\" deployment = both processes co-located on one machine sharing one filesystem, managed by one start script. |\n\n## 3. Verified current state (what we build on)\n\nThe split is substantially pre-architected:\n\n- **Portable route registry**: `src/runtime/http/routes/index.ts` registers all 62\n  method+path pairs framework-free (Fetch Request/Response). All 49 Next API\n  routes are thin adapters over it.\n- **Standalone server**: `src/runtime/http/server.ts` (`startOmniServer`) exists,\n  with `staticDir` support — but cannot stream (buffers `arrayBuffer()`), so the\n  two SSE endpoints (`/api/events`, `/api/terminals/:id/stream`) do not work\n  through it yet.\n- **Client transport abstraction**: `src/runtime-api/` (`RuntimeAPIs` interface;\n  `web.ts` already takes `baseUrl` + injectable `fetch`/`EventSource`).\n- **Non-Next entry**: `src/ui/render-web.tsx` mounts `<OmniApp>` without Next.\n- **Bootstrap over HTTP**: `GET /api/runtime/bootstrap` already mirrors the SSR\n  bootstrap for Electron/VSCode.\n- **Transports**: HTTP JSON + SSE only. No WebSockets, no server actions, no tRPC.\n\nKnown debts that gate the split (from exploration):\n\n- **(B) Streaming**: standalone server buffers responses; SSE endpoints hang.\n- **(C) Auth**: cookie + `SameSite=Lax` + `enforceSameOrigin` on all mutations +\n  zero CORS headers → a cross-origin interface cannot authenticate.\n- **(D) Runner identity**: `RuntimeAPIs` is a flat singleton; managers, SSE\n  cursor, and query caches assume \"the one server\".\n- **(H) 29 files** still hardcode `fetch('/api/...')`/`new EventSource('/api/...')`\n  instead of going through `RuntimeAPIs` (list in\n  `docs/architecture/common-runtime-multi-surface.md` §\"debt\").\n- **(G) Client→server imports**: `src/server/workers/entries-types.ts` (types),\n  `src/server/planning/review-preferences.ts` (runtime zod import) are imported\n  by client components.\n- **(A) Same-process event bus**: `src/server/events/named-events.ts` ring +\n  `live-updates.ts` listener set are module-level memory → the API server must\n  remain a single process. This is accepted and documented, not changed.\n\n## 4. Target architecture\n\n### 4.1 Repository shape (Approach A — in-place, two build targets)\n\nStay one pnpm package for now. Two build targets with an enforced boundary,\nno mass file moves:\n\n```\nsrc/server/       → runner only (never bundled into interface)\nsrc/runtime/      → runner only (HTTP registry, standalone server, bootstrap)\nsrc/shared/       → NEW: types + pure logic importable by both sides\nsrc/lib/          → interface-leaning isomorphic helpers (already clean)\nsrc/runtime-api/  → interface transport layer (multi-runner aware)\nsrc/ui/, src/components/, src/app/home/ → interface\napps/interface/   → NEW: Vite app (index.html, vite.config.ts, entry that\n                    reuses src/ui/render-web.tsx)\napps/electron/    → existing shell, becomes pure remote client\napps/vscode/      → unchanged\nscripts/runner.ts → NEW: runner entrypoint (replaces Next start for backend)\n```\n\n`src/app/` (Next) is deleted at the end of Milestone 2, except `src/app/home/`\nwhich is interface code and moves to `src/interface/home/` (import-path rename\nonly; no logic changes) so the `src/app` deletion is clean.\n\nBoundary enforcement:\n- ESLint `no-restricted-imports`: anything bundled by Vite may not import\n  `@/server/*` or `@/runtime/*` (only `@/shared/*`).\n- The two offending imports (G) move: `entries-types.ts` and\n  `review-preferences.ts` → `src/shared/`, with re-export shims left at the old\n  paths for server-side importers.\n- Vite build failing on a server import is the backstop.\n\n**Rejected alternatives**:\n- *Approach B — full monorepo package extraction* (`packages/shared|runner|interface`):\n  cleaner end state but moves ~460 files, breaks every open branch and all\n  `@/`-path muscle memory, and buys nothing functional now. Revisit after the\n  split is proven.\n- *Approach C — keep Next as frontend host*: rejected by user (RAM, second\n  pipeline).\n\n### 4.2 Runner\n\n**Entrypoint** `scripts/runner.ts`:\n1. `await dbReady` (schema init).\n2. Start supervisor watchdog (`ensureSupervisorRuntimeStarted()`).\n3. Start Claude model gateway (`ensureClaudeModelGatewayStartedAtBoot()` + shutdown hooks).\n4. Spawn/adopt the bridge exactly as `scripts/dev.ts` does today (lockfile,\n   health checks, `managed-bridge.ts`) — extracted into a shared module so\n   dev.ts and runner.ts don't duplicate it.\n5. `startOmniServer({ staticDir, port })` — one HTTP port for API + SSE + SPA.\n\nThis replaces `src/instrumentation.ts` (which is deleted with Next). Boot emits\nnamed events: `runtime.started` / `runtime.start_failed` already exist via\n`createOmniRuntime`; add `runner.bridge_ready`, `runner.static_ui_enabled`.\n\n**Streaming fix (prerequisite for everything)**: `writeFetchResponse` in\n`src/runtime/http/server.ts` pipes `Response.body` (Web `ReadableStream`) to the\nNode `ServerResponse` chunk-by-chunk, flushes headers immediately, disables\nNagle for `text/event-stream`, and destroys the upstream reader on client\ndisconnect. Non-streaming responses keep the buffered path.\n\n**SPA serving + bootstrap injection**: when `staticDir` is set, `GET /` (and the\n`/session/:runId` deep-link rewrite from `next.config.ts`) serve `index.html`\nwith `<script id=\"omni-bootstrap\" type=\"application/json\">…</script>` injected,\ncontaining the same payload as `buildRuntimeBootstrap` (auth session state,\nroute params, feature flags — `includeInitialData: false` semantics, matching\ntoday). Static assets get long-cache headers; `index.html` is `no-store`.\nThe interface reads the inline blob if present, else falls back to\n`GET /api/runtime/bootstrap` (Electron / remote / dev path).\n\n**Ports & processes** (unchanged topology, per user decision):\n- API server: default 3050 (today's web port), configurable.\n- Bridge: 7800, localhost-bound by default. Existing `OMNIHARNESS_*` env vars keep\n  their meanings. The dev compression proxy (3035) is retired with Next dev;\n  the VSCode default `serverUrl` moves to the runner port.\n- The `PORT`/host envs and `scripts/start.ts`, `controller.sh`, `omni.sh` are\n  updated to launch the runner instead of Next.\n\n### 4.3 Auth for remote interfaces\n\nToday: cookie `omni_session` + `enforceSameOrigin` on mutations + no CORS. This\nstays the default for the same-origin web/PWA path (served by the runner) — no\nbehavior change there.\n\nAdded for cross-origin clients (Electron, dev Vite server, future shells):\n\n1. **Bearer tokens.** `POST /api/auth/pair/redeem` (and `POST /api/auth/login`)\n   accept `{\"tokenTransport\":\"bearer\"}` and return the opaque session token in\n   the JSON body instead of (only) a Set-Cookie. Same `auth_sessions` table, same\n   idle/absolute expiry, new `transport` column value for audit. Guards\n   (`requireApiSession`) accept `Authorization: Bearer <token>` as an\n   alternative to the cookie; same-origin enforcement is skipped for\n   bearer-authenticated requests (the token *is* the CSRF defense — it is never\n   ambient).\n2. **SSE auth**: `EventSource` cannot set headers. Bearer clients open\n   `/api/events` and `/api/terminals/:id/stream` with a short-lived (60s,\n   single-use) stream ticket obtained from `POST /api/auth/stream-ticket`,\n   passed as `?ticket=`. Long-lived tokens never appear in URLs.\n3. **CORS**: explicit allowlist, default empty (= current behavior). Config via\n   settings/env (`OMNIHARNESS_CORS_ORIGINS`). Electron/VSCode don't need CORS\n   (no browser origin / extension-host proxy); the Vite dev server does\n   (`http://localhost:5173`) — dev.ts sets it automatically.\n4. **Pairing UX for remote devices**: existing QR flow already encodes a URL +\n   pair token. The interface's \"Add runner\" screen accepts pasted URL+token or\n   the QR deep link, calls `pair/redeem` with bearer transport, stores the\n   token in the runner profile.\n5. **Auth guards drop `next/server`**: `NextRequest` usage in\n   `auth/guards.ts`, `auth/session.ts`, `api-errors.ts` is replaced with plain\n   Fetch `Request` (the 9-line shim `routes/next-request.ts` shows this is\n   mechanical).\n\nTrust rule: if the API server binds to a non-loopback host and no password is\nconfigured, the runner logs a prominent warning and emits a named event\n(`auth.unprotected_non_loopback`) — same spirit as today, made explicit.\n\n### 4.4 Interface: Vite SPA\n\n- `apps/interface/` with Vite + React. Entry reuses `src/ui/render-web.tsx`;\n  `src/app/layout.tsx` content is ported to `index.html` (theme bootstrap\n  script, meta/manifest/icons) and a `<Providers>` root.\n- Remove Next-isms (all verified small):\n  - `next/dynamic` (4 files) → `React.lazy` + `Suspense`.\n  - `next/image` (5 files) → `<img>` (all usages are local static assets).\n  - `next/font/google` → self-hosted Geist via `@fontsource` (offline-friendly\n    for Electron/PWA; no Google fetch at build or runtime).\n  - `src/app/home/` → `src/interface/home/` (rename only).\n- PWA: `public/manifest.webmanifest`, `public/sw.js`, `PwaBootstrap` move into\n  the Vite `public/`, served by the runner → phone install keeps working per\n  runner origin, unchanged.\n- **Dev workflow**: `pnpm dev` = runner via `tsx watch` + Vite dev server with\n  proxy to the runner for `/api` (or CORS + absolute base URL — proxy preferred\n  so cookies keep working same-origin in dev). Expected dev RSS: runner is a\n  plain Node process; Vite replaces the 8 GB Next dev process.\n- **Prod web**: `vite build` output is what the runner serves. `pnpm build`\n  produces both (tsc for runner is unnecessary — runner runs via tsx today;\n  keep that, revisit bundling later).\n\n### 4.5 Multi-runner client model\n\nNew layer in `src/runtime-api/`:\n\n```ts\ntype RunnerProfile = {\n  id: string;            // stable uuid, client-generated\n  label: string;         // \"MacBook\", \"office-box\"\n  baseUrl: string;       // https://runner.tail1234.ts.net\n  authToken?: string;    // bearer; absent for same-origin default runner\n  createdAt: string; lastConnectedAt?: string;\n};\n```\n\n- **RunnerRegistry** (persisted): list of profiles + `activeRunnerId`.\n  Persistence: `localStorage` for web/PWA (per-origin; the implicit same-origin\n  runner is always present as profile id `\"local\"`), Electron `safeStorage`-\n  encrypted store for tokens on desktop. Settings dialog gets a \"Runners\"\n  section: add / edit / remove / test-connection / set-active.\n- **Per-runner connection scope.** Everything that is currently a module/app\n  singleton keyed to \"the server\" becomes owned by a `RunnerConnection` object\n  created per profile: `RuntimeAPIs` instance, `LiveEventConnectionManager`\n  (EventSource + `lastEventId` cursor), `EventStreamStateManager`, snapshot\n  cache, `WorkerEntriesManager`, notification manager. React context provides\n  the *active* connection; TanStack Query keys gain a `runnerId` prefix.\n  Switching runners swaps the context — background connections for inactive\n  runners are **closed** in v1 (no background SSE), except a lightweight\n  reachability ping for the switcher's status dots.\n- **State invariants** (per `client-server-state-invariants`):\n  - Ownership: all domain state belongs to the runner; the client persists only\n    runner profiles, UI preferences, and drafts.\n  - Provenance: every snapshot/event frame is tagged with `runnerId` at the\n    connection layer; reducers reject frames whose `runnerId` ≠ their scope\n    (guards against switch races).\n  - Freshness: on switch/reconnect, the existing snapshot+`Last-Event-ID` resume\n    protocol runs per runner; `stream.resync_required` handling is unchanged.\n  - Races to test: rapid runner switching mid-stream; stale mutation resolving\n    after a switch (mutations carry `runnerId`, results dropped if scope\n    changed); token revoked mid-session (401 → profile marked needs-reauth,\n    banner + re-pair CTA).\n\n### 4.6 The 29 direct-fetch call sites\n\nAll migrate to methods on the per-runner `RuntimeAPIs` (extending\n`src/runtime-api/types.ts` with the missing domains: runs, git, fs, attachments,\nprojects/memory, planning, accounts, notifications, terminals). Mechanical but\nwide; `useHomeMutations.ts` (1,269 lines) is split by domain as part of the\nmigration (conversations / runs / planning / settings) to stay under the\n1200-line rule. `InteractiveTerminal.tsx`'s `EventSource` goes through the\nconnection's ticket-aware stream opener.\n\n### 4.7 Filesystem semantics\n\nPaths shown in the UI (project pickers, file viewer, attachments) already flow\nthrough runner APIs (`/api/fs`, `/api/fs/files`, `/api/attachments`) — they are\n**runner paths** and keep working remotely. Two fixes:\n- Electron `native.chooseFolder()` (local dialog) is only offered when the\n  active runner is the local one; remote runners always use the `/api/fs`\n  browser dialog.\n- File-link opening (`project-file-links.ts`) targets the runner's editor only\n  for local runners; for remote runners, links open the in-app file viewer.\n\n### 4.8 Notifications\n\n- Web/PWA: web-push stays as-is — VAPID keys and subscriptions are per-runner\n  (each runner origin registers its own service worker). Works remotely today.\n- Electron remote: `RuntimeAPIs.native.notify` already exists; the Electron\n  shell subscribes to the active runner's named events for notification-worthy\n  kinds. Background (inactive-runner) notifications are out of scope for v1.\n\n## 5. User stories (v1 acceptance)\n\n1. **Lean local dev** — As the builder, I run `pnpm dev` and get runner + Vite;\n   combined RSS is a small fraction of today's; the web UI at the runner port\n   behaves identically to the current app. *(Acceptance: full existing e2e suite\n   passes against the runner-served SPA.)*\n2. **Headless runner** — I run `pnpm runner` on a box with no display; it serves\n   the API, SSE, and the built UI on one port; `omni` CLI and `tests/lifecycle`\n   drive it end-to-end with no browser.\n3. **Phone** — I open the runner URL on my phone (tailscale/tunnel), install the\n   PWA, pair via QR, and supervise agents; terminal, composer, notifications work.\n4. **Electron remote** — In the desktop app I add a second runner by URL +\n   pairing token, see both in the switcher with status dots, and switch between\n   them; each shows its own runs/conversations with correct resume after\n   reconnect.\n5. **Failure honesty** — When a runner is unreachable, I see an explicit offline\n   banner with the failing URL and error detail (not a spinner); reconnect\n   resumes the stream via `Last-Event-ID`; auth failures name the runner and\n   offer re-pairing.\n6. **Return visit** — Runner profiles, active selection, and UI preferences\n   survive app restart on web, PWA, and Electron.\n\nStatus-awareness: switcher shows per-runner state (connected / connecting /\noffline / needs-reauth / version-skew). Version skew: bootstrap payload gains\n`runnerVersion` + `apiRevision`; interface warns when major revisions differ.\n\nOnboarding: Electron first launch with no reachable local runner shows a\n\"Connect a runner\" screen (URL + token / QR paste) instead of an error. Web/PWA\nnever needs onboarding (served by its runner).\n\n## 6. Error transparency & instrumentation\n\n- Existing posture (full error details + stack traces to the frontend) is\n  preserved; `update_error` SSE frames and `app-errors.ts` flows unchanged.\n- New named events: `runner.bridge_ready`, `runner.static_ui_enabled`,\n  `auth.bearer_issued`, `auth.stream_ticket_issued` (audit),\n  `auth.unprotected_non_loopback`, plus existing `runtime.started/stopped`.\n- Client-side connection lifecycle is logged through the existing\n  `LiveEventConnectionManager` diagnostics; runner switcher surfaces last error\n  per profile verbatim.\n- Control plane: everything remains scriptable — the runner is *more* testable\n  headless (that's what `tests/lifecycle` already does). `omni` CLI gains\n  `--runner <url>` / token flags.\n\n## 7. Testing strategy\n\n- **Unit/integration (vitest)**: streaming server tests (SSE through\n  `startOmniServer`: heartbeat, resume via `Last-Event-ID`, client-disconnect\n  cleanup, backpressure); bearer + stream-ticket auth tests; CORS allowlist\n  tests; RunnerRegistry persistence + race tests (switch mid-stream, stale\n  mutation, 401 handling); bootstrap-injection test.\n- **Lifecycle suite** (`tests/lifecycle`) runs against `scripts/runner.ts`\n  instead of Next — this is the primary regression gate for the backend split.\n- **Playwright e2e**: existing suites repointed at the runner-served SPA; new\n  spec for add-runner/switch flows (two runner processes on different ports).\n- **Candidate agentic user-journey tests** (require approval before running):\n  (a) pair phone-sized viewport via QR against a fresh runner and send a\n  message; (b) Electron shell connects to a remote runner, switches, and\n  recovers from a runner restart mid-conversation.\n\n## 8. Milestones\n\n**M1 — Runner stands alone** *(backend, no UI change)*\nStreaming fix in `server.ts`; `scripts/runner.ts` with boot duties + bridge\nmanagement; `next/server` removed from auth/api-errors; lifecycle suite green\nagainst the runner. Next keeps serving the UI meanwhile (unchanged).\n\n**M2 — Interface on Vite, Next deleted**\n`apps/interface` Vite app; layout ported; Next-isms removed; bootstrap\ninjection; `pnpm dev` = runner + Vite; PWA assets served by runner; Playwright\ngreen; delete `src/app/api/*`, `page/layout/instrumentation`, `next.config.ts`,\nNext deps. `src/app/home/` → `src/interface/home/`.\n\n**M3 — Remote auth + transport migration**\nBearer tokens, stream tickets, CORS; shared types moved to `src/shared/` +\nESLint boundary; all 29 call sites through `RuntimeAPIs` (splitting\n`useHomeMutations.ts`); `useHomeQueries` keys runner-ready.\n\n**M4 — Multi-runner + Electron remote**\nRunnerRegistry + profiles persistence, switcher UI + status, per-runner\nconnection scoping, onboarding screen, Electron becomes pure remote client\n(native folder-picker gating, safeStorage tokens), version-skew warning.\n\nEach milestone leaves the app fully working; M1/M2 are independently shippable.\n\n## 9. Risks & trust surfaces\n\n- **Exposing runners on a network** is the point of this work — bearer auth is\n  mandatory on non-loopback binds (warning event otherwise), CORS defaults\n  closed, tokens encrypted at rest in Electron, never in URLs (tickets are\n  short-lived + single-use). Recommend tailscale/tunnels in docs rather than\n  raw port-forwarding.\n- **SSE through proxies/tunnels**: heartbeats exist; the streaming rewrite must\n  flush per-frame (test through the compression-proxy scenario that motivated\n  `dev-compression-proxy.ts`).\n- **Single-process API server** remains a hard constraint (in-memory event\n  bus/PTYs). Documented; horizontal scaling is explicitly a non-goal.\n- **Churn risk**: M3 touches 29 files across the UI. Mitigated by mechanical,\n  per-domain PRs and the untouched e2e suite as the bar.\n- **Electron regression risk**: shell currently boots an in-process runtime;\n  conversion to remote client changes its startup contract — covered by\n  `tests/electron` updates in M4.\n\n## 10. North star & future direction (context, not commitment)\n\nOne interface, installed anywhere (desktop app, phone home-screen, editor),\nshowing all your runners: merged cross-runner dashboard, background\nnotifications from inactive runners, runner health/resource panel,\nTauri/mobile-native shells, package extraction (Approach B) once boundaries\nhave settled, and possibly runner-to-runner work handoff.\n\n## 11. Open questions for review\n\n1. Naming: \"runner\" vs existing \"bridge/agent-runtime\" vocabulary — spec uses\n   *runner* = API server + bridge pair; rename in docs only, not in code paths.\n2. `pnpm dev` proxy vs CORS for Vite dev (spec picks proxy; either works).\n3. Is the compression proxy (3035) still needed once Next dev is gone? Spec\n   assumes no; VSCode default port changes accordingly.\n"
}

## User · 2026-07-29T11:54:42.952Z

Base directory for this skill: /Users/masterman/.claude/skills/second-opinion

# Second Opinion

Use an external headless coding harness as a persistent reviewer while shaping substantial plans and specs.

## When To Use

Use this skill for:

- any saved implementation plan produced through `writing-plans` (mandatory),
- substantial specs where ambiguity, architecture, product scope, or implementation risk remains,
- unusually hard or creative design decisions where another independent pass could improve the result.

Skip it for tiny mechanical edits, obvious one-task changes, or short plans that are not being saved as durable execution artifacts.

## Core Rule

Run the second opinion through a separate reviewer session. Prefer a different available coding harness from the one currently writing the plan or spec because cross-harness review catches more blind spots than asking the same agent family to review its own artifact.

Different harnesses are preferred, not required. The second opinion itself is required for matching artifacts. If no different harness is available, using the same harness in a separate headless/session context is fine.

There is no budget limit for second opinions. Never add, infer, suggest, or pass a token, time, cost, or pass budget for second-opinion review.

Preferred pairings:

- Codex asks Claude Code when `claude` is available.
- Claude Code asks Codex CLI when `codex` is available.
- Gemini asks Codex or Claude Code when either is available.
- OpenCode asks Codex or Claude Code when either is available.
- Other harnesses ask the best available different coding harness.

If the preferred different harness is missing, use any other installed headless coding CLI (`gemini`, `opencode`, etc.) before falling back to the same harness. Same-harness review is acceptable when no different headless reviewer is available; say that explicitly and continue with the required second opinion.

Do not substitute an in-process self-review, browser chat, or a non-coding assistant unless the user explicitly asks for that. The point is a separate process with its own model, tool defaults, and context.

## Session Protocol

1. Identify the current harness.
2. Check for a different installed headless coding CLI, starting with the preferred pairing above.
3. Resolve commands with `command -v`; never guess an absolute executable path.
4. Confirm the installed version's headless, permission, output, and resume flags with `--help`.
5. Put the complete review prompt and artifact content in a prompt file unless the reviewer genuinely needs workspace context. Explicitly say not to inspect or edit files.
6. Run the reviewer from `PATH` with tools disabled when all context is inline. Otherwise grant only read access to the supplied artifact.
7. Wait for a complete written response. Ordinary runtime is not failure; keep waiting and give the user brief progress updates.
8. Accept the pass only when the final output contains the requested `READY` or `HARDEN` verdict and written findings.
9. If the host blocks the reviewer's authentication or state directory, request the required host permission and rerun the exact command unchanged.
10. If the command still fails, exits without a valid verdict, returns blank output, or returns only a tool request, move immediately to the next installed harness.
11. Capture the session id or thread handle when available, and resume that session for later passes.
12. Incorporate useful feedback, ignore conflicting or out-of-scope advice, and rerun after material revisions.
13. Stop when the reviewer says the artifact is ready, when the user explicitly waives the second-opinion gate, or when no separate reviewer surface is available.

Do not convert repeated reviewer passes into a fixed review budget. If the reviewer keeps finding material concerns, keep addressing them until the artifact is ready or ask the user how they want to proceed.

## Harness Discovery

Use `command -v` or the platform equivalent to check for reviewer CLIs before choosing a fallback. Do not install a new harness just for a second opinion unless the user asks.

Typical checks:

```bash
command -v claude || true
command -v codex || true
command -v gemini || true
command -v opencode || true
```

Invoke the discovered command by name. Do not invent paths under `/Applications`, package-manager directories, or a different user's home directory. A command present on another machine is not evidence that the same absolute path exists here.

Prefer read-only or plan-mode permissions for the reviewer. The reviewer should inspect and comment, not mutate files.

## Claude Code Pattern

For Claude Code, discover the current flags with `claude --help`. When the prompt file already contains the artifact, use a clean, tool-free review:

```bash
claude \
  --model sonnet \
  --permission-mode plan \
  --safe-mode \
  --tools "" \
  --output-format text \
  -p "$(cat /tmp/second-opinion-prompt.txt)" \
  > /tmp/second-opinion-pass-1.txt
```

Do not point this tool-free command at an artifact path it cannot read. Put the artifact text in the prompt file. If the artifact is too large to inline, allow only the `Read` tool and provide the exact artifact path.

If the installed Claude Code version exposes resume/session flags, use them for later passes. Otherwise, include the previous review summary and revised artifact in the next headless prompt.

## Codex CLI Pattern

For Codex, prefer a non-ephemeral session so it can be resumed:

```bash
codex exec \
  -C "$PWD" \
  --sandbox read-only \
  --skip-git-repo-check \
  --ignore-user-config \
  --json \
  -o /tmp/second-opinion-pass-1.txt \
  - < /tmp/second-opinion-prompt.txt
```

Capture the session id from the JSONL event stream when available. Resume the same reviewer:

```bash
codex exec resume \
  "$SESSION_ID" \
  --ignore-user-config \
  --json \
  -o /tmp/second-opinion-pass-N.txt \
  - < /tmp/second-opinion-followup.txt
```

If the CLI version exposes a different headless/resume shape, follow that version's `--help` output.

`--sandbox read-only` controls the reviewer's workspace access; the Codex process may still need normal host access to its own state database under `CODEX_HOME`. If the outer host blocks that state access, rerun the same command through the host's permission mechanism rather than changing paths or weakening the reviewer's workspace sandbox.

## Gemini CLI Pattern

For Gemini, prefer non-interactive prompt mode with plan/read-only approval when available:

```bash
gemini --prompt "$(cat /tmp/second-opinion-prompt.txt)" --approval-mode plan --output-format text > /tmp/second-opinion-pass-1.txt
```

Resume the same session with `--resume` if that is available and useful for later passes.

## Review Prompt Template

```text
You are my second-opinion planning reviewer. Use only the content in this prompt. Do not inspect or edit files and do not call tools.

User goal: [brief goal]
Context and constraints:
- [important constraints, user preferences, repo defaults]

Artifact:
[paste the complete plan or spec here]

Please judge whether this plan/spec is ready to use.

Look for:
- missing requirements or user instructions,
- vague tasks, placeholders, fake components, mocks, or fallback behavior posing as final work,
- scope creep or non-obvious product decisions that need user confirmation,
- missing file map, ownership, state, persistence, instrumentation, error, or verification details,
- sequencing problems or tasks that are too large to execute safely,
- test gaps and unclear acceptance criteria.

Return:
1. Verdict: READY or HARDEN
2. Highest-impact findings, with concrete fixes
3. Anything you would deliberately leave alone
```

## Handling Feedback

Treat the reviewer as advisory, not authoritative. Apply feedback when it improves correctness, completeness, clarity, safety, or testability. Do not apply feedback that conflicts with the user, local repo instructions, or already-approved scope.

The reviewer feedback does not need to be copied into the plan or spec. Keep the durable artifact clean unless the user explicitly asks for review notes.

## Completion And Fallback Gate

| Result | Action |
|---|---|
| Written `READY` or `HARDEN` verdict with findings | Accept the pass |
| Explicit authentication or state-permission error | Rerun the exact command with the host permission it requested |
| Nonzero exit, blank output, partial output, missing verdict, or tool-only response | Try the next installed harness |
| Reviewer is still working normally | Keep waiting; do not mistake duration for failure |

Exit code zero alone does not complete the review. A JSON tool request, an attempted file search, or prose without the requested verdict is not a written review.

## Failure Mode

Follow the fallback chain until one harness returns a valid written verdict. If no different headless coding harness succeeds, say that explicitly and use the current harness headlessly if possible. Same-harness review still satisfies the required second-opinion gate when it runs as a separate reviewer session. If no separate reviewer surface is available at all, stop and say the mandatory second opinion cannot be run; do not replace it with an in-process self-review unless the user explicitly waives the gate.

Single-use SSE tickets conflict with EventSource reconnection\n\n`EventSource` automatically reconnects to the same URL. Once its single-use ticket has been consumed, that URL cannot reconnect. Creating a new `EventSource` with a fresh ticket also loses native `Last-Event-ID` state unless the cursor is carried explicitly.\n\nChoose and specify one solution:\n\n- Prefer authenticated `fetch()` streaming with `Authorization` and an SSE parser, eliminating stream tickets; or\n- Make `LiveEventConnectionManager` own reconnection: close the failed EventSource, obtain a fresh path-scoped ticket, create a new EventSource with the saved cursor in the URL, and suppress native retry behavior.\n\nTickets should be atomically consumed, short-lived, bound to the session and exact stream path, excluded from logs, and accepted only over HTTPS except on loopback.\n\nTests must include reconnect after ticket consumption, ticket expiry, concurrent redemption, terminal streams, and cursor resume without duplicate events.\n\n### 3. Electron’s security boundary is not actually designed\n\n“Tokens encrypted with `safeStorage`” only protects data while it is on disk. If the token is handed to the renderer so `RuntimeAPIs` can make requests, renderer compromise exposes it. The statement that Electron does not need CORS is also only reliable if networking happens outside the renderer.\n\nConcrete fix:\n\n- Keep profile tokens and remote HTTP/SSE traffic in the Electron main process.\n- Expose a narrow IPC-backed `RuntimeAPIs` implementation to the renderer.\n- Return parsed responses/events, never bearer tokens.\n- Document navigation restrictions, external-link handling, IPC validation, and token redaction.\n- If renderer-side networking is intentionally retained, state its origin/CORS requirements and acknowledge that `safeStorage` is only at-rest protection.\n\n### 4. Client profile IDs are not runner identity\n\nA client-generated profile UUID identifies a saved connection, not the server. The design cannot reliably detect duplicate profiles, a URL redirected to a different runner, or a profile URL edited while retaining a token.\n\nAdd a durable server-generated `runnerInstanceId` to bootstrap, stored with runner state, plus a display name and capability flags. After pairing:\n\n- Save the expected `runnerInstanceId`.\n- Reject or require confirmation when the URL later resolves to another identity.\n- Clear credentials whenever `baseUrl` changes.\n- Deduplicate profiles pointing to the same runner.\n- Never forward authorization across cross-origin redirects.\n\nAlso, profile ID `\"local\"` is not proof that the runner shares the Electron machine or filesystem. Same-origin in a browser may still mean a remote host. Native folder picking and editor opening should require an explicit co-location capability established by the Electron-launched runner, not a label or localhost heuristic.\n\n### 5. PWA caching and bootstrap injection can leak or preserve stale state\n\nThe injected `index.html` contains session-specific bootstrap data and is marked `no-store`, but a service worker can still cache it explicitly. That risks replaying stale authentication state and makes offline behavior unclear.\n\nConcrete fix:\n\n- Never put injected `index.html` into the service-worker cache.\n- Provide a separate static, non-personalized offline app shell that falls back to `/api/runtime/bootstrap`.\n- Define service-worker versioning, asset eviction, navigation fallback, and behavior when the runner that originally hosted the PWA is offline.\n- Clarify notifications: one installed PWA has one service worker origin. It does not register a service worker “per remote runner.” Its push subscription may be registered with several runners, with runner identity included in each notification.\n\nAdd offline launch, upgrade, stale-cache, and multi-runner push tests.\n\n### 6. Network security is internally contradictory\n\nSection 4.3 says a non-loopback unauthenticated runner only logs a warning. Section 9 says bearer authentication is mandatory on non-loopback binds. Those are different policies.\n\nThe safer executable rule is:\n\n- Refuse non-loopback startup without authentication unless an explicit `--allow-insecure-network` or equivalent setting is supplied.\n- Refuse persistent bearer authentication over plaintext non-loopback HTTP, or require an equally explicit insecure override.\n- Accept HTTP for loopback development.\n- Rate-limit login, pairing redemption, and ticket issuance.\n- Redact authorization headers, pairing tokens, stream tickets, and token-bearing URLs from logs and error reports.\n\nThis needs user confirmation because it changes current permissive behavior.\n\n### 7. The production artifact does not yet satisfy “no frontend toolchain”\n\nThe runner still executes TypeScript using `tsx`, while `pnpm build` apparently only guarantees a Vite bundle. It is unclear what gets installed on a headless machine, whether Vite and its dependencies are present, or what happens when the static bundle is missing.\n\nDefine the artifact:\n\n- `dist/runner/` containing checked/typechecked runnable backend code.\n- `dist/interface/` containing hashed static assets.\n- A production start command that does not run Vite, Next, watchers, or frontend compilation.\n- Clear API-only behavior when `dist/interface` is intentionally absent, and a clear startup failure when it is expected but missing.\n- Separate no-emit typechecks for runner, shared code, and interface even if the runner continues using `tsx`.\n\n“Small fraction of today’s RSS” also needs a reproducible workload and threshold, including steady state and repeated rebuilds.\n\n### 8. Static serving needs a precise contract\n\nInjection and fallback behavior are security-sensitive and broader than `/session/:runId`.\n\nSpecify:\n\n- Safe JSON serialization that prevents `</script>` termination and escapes `<`, U+2028, and U+2029.\n- CSP behavior for the injected script.\n- SPA fallback for known navigation requests or extensionless HTML requests.\n- `/api/*` and missing asset paths must never fall through to `index.html`.\n- Path traversal and symlink rules.\n- Immutable caching only for content-hashed assets; `index.html` remains `no-store`.\n- Route parity tests covering status codes, headers, cookies, multipart bodies, SSE, and errors before deleting the Next adapters.\n\n### 9. Milestones 3 and 4 are too large and hide partially working states\n\nM3 combines a security protocol, CORS, shared-boundary cleanup, 29 transport migrations, and a major hook split. Standalone runner, streaming, readiness/shutdown, and route-parity tests.\n2. Vite SPA served by runner; full e2e parity; then delete Next.\n3. Complete `RuntimeAPIs` migration and enforce the client/server import boundary.\n4. Add stable runner identity and the remote auth/CORS protocol behind a disabled multi-runner feature.\n5. Introduce per-runner connection ownership and race tests.\n6. Add registry/switcher/onboarding.\n7. Convert Electron networking and secret storage.\n8. Enable the feature after two-runner browser and Electron e2e tests pass.\n\nEach intermediate commit should keep remote multi-runner disabled until every request path uses the scoped transport.\n\n### 10. A few acceptance contracts remain vague\n\nAdd explicit behavior for:\n\n- Liveness versus readiness, bridge unavailable at startup, bridge crash/restart, port conflicts, and graceful shutdown ordering.\n- Status-dot ping endpoint, timeouts, backoff, and authentication.\n- Duplicate run IDs when switching runners, URL/deep-link ownership, local drafts, and mutations that finish after switching. Dropping the result does not undo the remote mutation; the completion should remain attributed to its originating runner.\n- API compatibility: publish supported minimum/maximum API revisions. Block known-incompatible runners instead of merely warning about unspecified “major” differences.\n- Profile storage schema versioning, corruption recovery, token revocation, profile removal, and base-URL edits.\n- Two-runner tests using separate databases, filesystems, ports, identities, and credentials.\n- VSCode authentication and port migration; “unchanged” should mean behavior remains unchanged, not that no code or tests need updating.\n\n## 3. What I would deliberately leave alone\n\nI would keep these decisions as written:\n\n- One package with two enforced build targets rather than an immediate monorepo extraction.\n- The API server and bridge as separate co-located processes.\n- The single-process API constraint caused by the in-memory event bus and PTYs.\n- One active runner at a time, with inactive SSE connections closed.\n- Vite’s development proxy as the normal local workflow.\n- Same-origin cookies for the runner-served web path and bearer authentication for remote clients.\n- Migrating all direct fetches into `RuntimeAPIs`, including splitting the oversized mutations hook.\n- Removing Next only after route and UI parity are proven.\n- Keeping full frontend error detail, provided credentials, tickets, and sensitive headers are always redacted.\n- Deferring merged dashboards, inactive-runner Electron notifications, Tauri, horizontal scaling, and package extraction.\n\nWith the cross-origin policy, stream reconnection model, Electron boundary, stable runner identity, and PWA caching contract resolved, this becomes ready to turn into an implementation plan."}}
