## Claude thinking · 2026-07-29T11:59:49.355Z

I'm reviewing the HARDEN findings and triaging which ones apply to the current work. The first critical issue is CORS with the PWA multi-runner setup—when a PWA installed from one runner calls another, it hits cross-origin restrictions that break the flow. I need to implement origin-bound bearer sessions and set up pair/redeem logic to allow cross-runner communication.

For the SSE ticket problem, the LiveEventConnectionManager already handles reconnection and cursor tracking, so I can specify that it fetches a fresh ticket on each (re)connect and passes the cursor via the existing ?lastEventId parameter—avoiding reliance on native EventSource retry logic.

The Electron token boundary is reasonable to address by implementing a main-process proxy pattern similar to VSCode's bridge approach. I'm also applying the server-generated runnerInstanceId for better runner identity tracking, plus adding co-location capability as a moderate improvement over the current local heuristic.

For PWA caching, the service worker should never cache index.html and fall back to an offline shell instead. The push notification constraint is trickier—since subscriptions are tied to the VAPID keypair used at subscribe time, a single PWA can only have one push subscription per service worker registration, which means multi-runner push notifications either need shared VAPID keys or routing through a home runner. The simplest v1 approach is to limit push notifications to a single runner initially.

There's also a security contradiction between the spec sections on warnings versus mandatory auth—I'll resolve this by refusing non-loopback binds without authentication unless an explicit override flag is set, and note it as a recommended default with an environment variable override. For production artifacts, I'm defining the dist layout where `pnpm build` generates the interface in dist/, and the runner continues using tsx since it's lightweight enough for production.

On resource usage, I need to make the "small footprint" claim measurable—I'll add a script to measure local dev performance and set a concrete target like keeping combined dev under 2GB. For static serving, I'm applying security contracts around JSON escaping, preventing SPA fallback for API routes, using immutable caching for hashed assets, and handling path traversal properly.

The reviewer also flagged that the milestone structure should be resequenced into 6 stages, with multi-runner support gated behind a feature flag until the transport migration finishes. On acceptance criteria, I'm adding readiness/liveness checks, a status ping spec, clarifying mutation semantics after switching (results attributed, not dropped), API versioning bounds, profile schema versioning, two-runner test isolation, and VSCode extension updates—though I'll keep this selective to avoid bloating the spec.

Now I'm updating the spec with these changes, then I'll rerun the reviewer to validate the material revisions before moving forward.

Codex found several critical issues: CORS defaults conflict with the multi-runner architecture, SSE tickets break reconnection, Electron token handling needs better boundaries, client UUIDs don't establish runner identity, and there's a contradiction between warning and mandatory auth requirements. I'm revising the spec to address these.

Backend runs as a lean process that does not also host a frontend toolchain\n   (Next dev today balloons to 8 GB+ RSS).\n3. Web version keeps working exactly as it does now (same URL shape, same UI).\n\n## 2. Decisions already made (user-confirmed)\n\n| Decision | Choice |\n|---|---|\n| Next.js | **Remove.** Interface becomes a Vite SPA; runner serves the built bundle and injects the bootstrap JSON into `index.html` at serve time (preserves today's zero-round-trip boot; verified that the app tree is `ssr:false` and SSR only emits the BootShell skeleton + bootstrap blob). |\n| Native shells v1 | **Electron** (existing `apps/electron`, converted to pure remote client). Phone = existing PWA support, now served by the runner. VSCode shell behavior unchanged. Tauri: future option, out of scope. |\n| Multi-runner UI v1 | **Runner switcher**: save N runner profiles, connect, switch; one runner's data on screen at a time. Merged cross-runner views: future. |\n| Backend topology | **Keep two services** as today: the omni API server and the agent-runtime bridge (port 7800) stay separate processes. Clients only ever talk to the API server; the bridge keeps binding to localhost by default (`OMNIHARNESS_AGENT_RUNTIME_HOST`). A \"runner\" deployment = both processes co-located on one machine sharing one filesystem, managed by one start script. |\n\n## 3. Verified current state (what we build on)\n\nThe split is substantially pre-architected:\n\n- **Portable route registry**: `src/runtime/http/routes/index.ts` registers all 62\n  method+path pairs framework-free (Fetch Request/Response). All 49 Next API\n  routes are thin adapters over it.\n- **Standalone server**: `src/runtime/http/server.ts` (`startOmniServer`) exists,\n  with `staticDir` support — but cannot stream (buffers `arrayBuffer()`), so the\n  two SSE endpoints (`/api/events`, `/api/terminals/:id/stream`) do not work\n  through it yet.\n- **Client transport abstraction**: `src/runtime-api/` (`RuntimeAPIs` interface;\n  `web.ts` already takes `baseUrl` + injectable `fetch`/`EventSource`).\n- **Non-Next entry**: `src/ui/render-web.tsx` mounts `<OmniApp>` without Next.\n- **Bootstrap over HTTP**: `GET /api/runtime/bootstrap` already mirrors the SSR\n  bootstrap for Electron/VSCode.\n- **Transports**: HTTP JSON + SSE only. No WebSockets, no server actions, no tRPC.\n\nKnown debts that gate the split (from exploration):\n\n- **(B) Streaming**: standalone server buffers responses; SSE endpoints hang.\n- **(C) Auth**: cookie + `SameSite=Lax` + `enforceSameOrigin` on all mutations +\n  zero CORS headers → a cross-origin interface cannot authenticate.\n- **(D) Runner identity**: `RuntimeAPIs` is a flat singleton; managers, SSE\n  cursor, and query caches assume \"the one server\".\n- **(H) 29 files** still hardcode `fetch('/api/...')`/`new EventSource('/api/...')`\n  instead of going through `RuntimeAPIs` (list in\n  `docs/architecture/common-runtime-multi-surface.md` §\"debt\").\n- **(G) Client→server imports**: `src/server/workers/entries-types.ts` (types),\n  `src/server/planning/review-preferences.ts` (runtime zod import) are imported\n  by client components.\n- **(A) Same-process event bus**: `src/server/events/named-events.ts` ring +\n  `live-updates.ts` listener set are module-level memory → the API server must\n  remain a single process. This is accepted and documented, not changed.\n\n## 4. Target architecture\n\n### 4.1 Repository shape (Approach A — in-place, two build targets)\n\nStay one pnpm package for now. Two build targets with an enforced boundary,\nno mass file moves:\n\n```\nsrc/server/       → runner only (never bundled into interface)\nsrc/runtime/      → runner only (HTTP registry, standalone server, bootstrap)\nsrc/shared/       → NEW: types + pure logic importable by both sides\nsrc/lib/          → interface-leaning isomorphic helpers (already clean)\nsrc/runtime-api/  → interface transport layer (multi-runner aware)\nsrc/ui/, src/components/, src/interface/home/ → interface\napps/interface/   → NEW: Vite app (index.html, vite.config.ts, entry that\n                    reuses src/ui/render-web.tsx)\napps/electron/    → existing shell, becomes pure remote client\napps/vscode/      → behavior unchanged; port/auth wiring updated\nscripts/runner.ts → NEW: runner entrypoint (replaces Next start for backend)\n```\n\n`src/app/` (Next) is deleted at the end of Milestone 2; `src/app/home/` is\ninterface code and moves to `src/interface/home/` (import-path rename only) so\nthe `src/app` deletion is clean.\n\nBoundary enforcement:\n- ESLint `no-restricted-imports`: anything bundled by Vite may not import\n  `@/server/*` or `@/runtime/*` (only `@/shared/*`).\n- The two offending imports (G) move: `entries-types.ts` and\n  `review-preferences.ts` → `src/shared/`, with re-export shims left at the old\n  paths for server-side importers.\n- Vite build failing on a server import is the backstop.\n- Typechecking is split: separate `tsc --noEmit` projects for runner\n  (`src/server` + `src/runtime`), shared, and interface, so a server-only type\n  error can't hide in the SPA project and vice versa.\n\n**Production artifact contract** (what a headless box installs):\n- `pnpm build` → `dist/interface/` (hashed static assets + `index.html`\n  template). The runner itself keeps running from source via `tsx`\n  (existing convention in `scripts/start.ts`); no Vite, Next, or watcher runs\n  in production.\n- Runner with `dist/interface` present → serves SPA + API. Absent → **API-only\n  mode**: `/` returns a plain informational page, a named event\n  `runner.static_ui_missing` is emitted, and everything else works (this is a\n  supported deployment, not an error). If `--static-dir` is passed explicitly\n  and missing, startup fails loudly.\n- Memory acceptance: measured via `scripts/measure-local-dev.mjs` —\n  dev (runner + Vite) steady-state combined RSS **< 2 GB**, production runner\n  steady-state **< 1 GB**, both measured after a scripted 10-minute\n  conversation workload. (Today's Next dev alone exceeds 8 GB.)\n\n**Rejected alternatives**:\n- *Approach B — full monorepo package extraction* (`packages/shared|runner|interface`):\n  cleaner end state but moves ~460 files, breaks every open branch, buys\n  nothing functional now. Revisit after the split is proven.\n- *Approach C — keep Next as frontend host*: rejected by user (RAM, second\n  pipeline).\n\n### 4.2 Runner\n\n**Entrypoint** `scripts/runner.ts`:\n1. `await dbReady` (schema init).\n2. Start supervisor watchdog (`ensureSupervisorRuntimeStarted()`).\n3. Start Claude model gateway (`ensureClaudeModelGatewayStartedAtBoot()` + shutdown hooks).\n4. Spawn/adopt the bridge exactly as `scripts/dev.ts` does today (lockfile,\n   health checks, `managed-bridge.ts`) — extracted into a shared module so\n   dev.ts and runner.ts don't duplicate it.\n5. `startOmniServer({ staticDir, port })` — one HTTP port for API + SSE + SPA.\n\nThis replaces `src/instrumentation.ts` (deleted with Next).\n\n**Lifecycle contract**:\n- `GET /api/healthz` (liveness, no auth, no state) and readiness reflected in\n  the existing bootstrap route. Bridge unavailable at boot → runner starts,\n  serves UI/API, reports `bridge: \"starting\" | \"unavailable\"` in bootstrap and\n  emits `runner.bridge_unavailable`; bridge crash → existing supervisor\n  sync/retry behavior, surfaced as named events (`runner.bridge_ready` on\n  (re)connect). Port conflict → fail fast with the owning pid (reuse\n  bridge-lock diagnostics pattern). Shutdown order: stop accepting new\n  requests → end SSE streams with a final `stream.resync_required`-style\n  goodbye frame → terminals/PTYs → gateway child → release bridge lock.\n\n**Streaming fix (prerequisite for everything)**: `writeFetchResponse` in\n`src/runtime/http/server.ts` pipes `Response.body` (Web `ReadableStream`) to the\nNode `ServerResponse` chunk-by-chunk, flushes headers immediately, disables\nNagle for `text/event-stream`, and destroys the upstream reader on client\ndisconnect. Non-streaming responses keep the buffered path.\n\n**Static serving + bootstrap injection contract**:\n- `GET /` and `/session/:runId` (the deep-link rewrite from `next.config.ts`)\n  serve `index.html` with\n  `<script id=\"omni-bootstrap\" type=\"application/json\">…</script>` injected —\n  same payload semantics as today's SSR (`includeInitialData: false`). JSON is\n  serialized with `<`, `>`, `&`, U+2028, U+2029 escaped so it cannot terminate\n  the script element.\n- SPA fallback applies only to GET navigations without a file extension that\n  aren't `/api/*`. `/api/*` and missing assets return proper 404s — never\n  `index.html`.\n- Hashed assets: `Cache-Control: public, max-age=31536000, immutable`.\n  `index.html`: `no-store` (it contains per-request auth state).\n- Static file resolution rejects path traversal and does not follow symlinks\n  out of `staticDir`.\n- The interface reads the inline blob if present, else falls back to\n  `GET /api/runtime/bootstrap` (Electron / remote / dev / offline-PWA path).\n\n**Ports & processes** (unchanged topology, per user decision):\n- API server: default 3050 (today's web port), configurable.\n- Bridge: 7800, localhost-bound by default. Existing `OMNIHARNESS_*` env vars\n  keep their meanings. The dev compression proxy (3035) is retired with Next\n  dev; the VSCode default `serverUrl` moves to the runner port.\n- `scripts/start.ts`, `controller.sh`, `omni.sh` launch the runner instead of\n  Next.\n\n### 4.3 Auth & cross-origin model\n\nTwo session transports against the same `auth_sessions` table and expiry rules:\n\n**Cookie sessions (unchanged)** — for the interface served by the runner itself\n(web + PWA on that runner's origin). `SameSite=Lax`, `enforceSameOrigin` on\nmutations, no CORS involvement. No behavior change.\n\n**Bearer sessions (new)** — for every cross-origin client: the PWA installed\nfrom runner A talking to runner B, Electron, the Vite dev server, future\nshells.\n\n1. **Issuance**: `POST /api/auth/pair/redeem` and `POST /api/auth/login` accept\n   `{\"tokenTransport\":\"bearer\"}` and return the opaque session token in the\n   response body. New `transport` column in `auth_sessions` for audit. Guards\n   accept `Authorization: Bearer <token>`; same-origin enforcement is skipped\n   for bearer requests (the token is never ambient, so it is the CSRF defense).\n2. **Origin binding (this is the multi-runner CORS design)**: browsers enforce\n   CORS, so runner B must answer the PWA-from-runner-A origin. Policy:\n   - `POST /api/auth/pair/redeem` and `/api/auth/login` are callable from\n     **any** origin (`Access-Control-Allow-Origin: <reflected>`, no\n     credentials mode) — protected by the expiring single-use pairing token /\n     password + existing rate limiting (`auth/rate-limit.ts`).\n   - A bearer session created cross-origin is **bound to the redeeming\n     `Origin`**. Subsequent bearer requests reflect exactly that bound origin\n     (`Vary: Origin`), and a bearer token presented from a different origin is\n     rejected (401, audited). Non-browser clients (Electron main process,\n     VSCode host, CLI — no `Origin` header) are exempt from binding.\n   - Preflight: `OPTIONS` handled registry-wide for bearer routes\n     (`Allow-Headers: authorization, content-type`, methods per route,\n     `Access-Control-Max-Age` set). Credentialed wildcard CORS is never used;\n     cookie routes never get CORS headers.\n   - No static allowlist config needed for the core story; an optional\n     `OMNIHARNESS_CORS_ORIGINS` allowlist can additionally enable\n     cookie-less same-device conveniences in dev.\n3. **SSE auth**: `EventSource` cannot set headers. Bearer clients call\n   `POST /api/auth/stream-ticket` → short-lived (60 s), **single-use,\n   atomically consumed**, bound to (session, exact stream path); passed as\n   `?ticket=`. Reconnection is owned by `LiveEventConnectionManager` (it\n   already owns retry + `Last-Event-ID` bookkeeping): native `EventSource`\n   auto-retry is disabled (`close()` on error), and each (re)connect fetches a\n   fresh ticket and passes the saved cursor via the existing `?lastEventId=`\n   parameter. Tickets never appear in logs (redaction in the request logger);\n   accepted only over HTTPS except on loopback. Same mechanism for\n   `/api/terminals/:id/stream`.\n4. **Pairing UX for remote devices**: existing QR flow already encodes URL +\n   pair token; the \"Add runner\" screen accepts the QR deep link or pasted\n   URL+token, redeems with bearer transport, stores the token in the profile.\n5. **Network exposure policy** (changes current permissive behavior —\n   flagged for user confirmation): binding the API server to a non-loopback\n   host with no auth configured **refuses to start** unless\n   `OMNIHARNESS_ALLOW_UNAUTHENTICATED_NETWORK=1` is set; bearer issuance over\n   plaintext non-loopback HTTP is likewise refused without the same explicit\n   override. Loopback HTTP stays frictionless. `auth.unprotected_non_loopback`\n   named event is emitted whenever the override is used.\n6. **Redaction**: `Authorization` headers, pair tokens, stream tickets, and\n   token-bearing URLs are scrubbed from logs, error payloads, and named-event\n   details.\n7. **Next removal**: `NextRequest` usage in `auth/guards.ts`,\n   `auth/session.ts`, `api-errors.ts` is replaced with plain Fetch `Request`\n   (the 9-line shim `routes/next-request.ts` shows this is mechanical).\n\n### 4.4 Interface: Vite SPA\n\n- `apps/interface/` with Vite + React. Entry reuses `src/ui/render-web.tsx`;\n  `src/app/layout.tsx` content is ported to `index.html` (theme bootstrap\n  script, meta/manifest/icons) and a `<Providers>` root.\n- Remove Next-isms (all verified small):\n  - `next/dynamic` (4 files) → `React.lazy` + `Suspense`.\n  - `next/image` (5 files) → `<img>` (all usages are local static assets).\n  - `next/font/google` → self-hosted Geist via `@fontsource` (offline-friendly,\n    no Google fetch at build or runtime).\n  - `src/app/home/` → `src/interface/home/` (rename only).\n- **PWA & service worker contract**:\n  - `manifest.webmanifest`, `sw.js`, `PwaBootstrap` move to the Vite app,\n    served by the runner → phone install keeps working per runner origin.\n  - The SW **never caches `index.html`** (it carries per-request auth state).\n    It precaches hashed assets plus a static, non-personalized offline shell\n    that boots by calling `/api/runtime/bootstrap`; offline launch shows the\n    shell with an explicit \"runner unreachable\" state, not a cached session.\n  - SW versioning: build hash in the SW file; old caches evicted on activate.\n- **Dev workflow**: `pnpm dev` = runner via `tsx watch` + Vite dev server\n  proxying `/api` to the runner (cookies stay same-origin in dev; no CORS\n  needed). Expected dev RSS per §4.1 contract.\n- **Prod web**: `vite build` output is what the runner serves.\n\n### 4.5 Multi-runner client model\n\n**Runner identity is server-generated.** The bootstrap payload (inline and\n`/api/runtime/bootstrap`) gains:\n\n```ts\nrunner: {\n  instanceId: string;   // generated once, stored in the settings table\n  name: string;         // user-configurable display name\n  version: string;      // package version\n  apiRevision: number;  // monotonically bumped on breaking API change\n}\n```\n\nClient-side profile:\n\n```ts\ntype RunnerProfile = {\n  id: string;                 // client uuid for list keys only — NOT identity\n  runnerInstanceId?: string;  // learned at first successful connect/pair\n  label: string; baseUrl: string;\n  authToken?: string;         // bearer; absent for the same-origin runner\n  schemaVersion: 1;\n  createdAt: string; lastConnectedAt?: string;\n};\n```\n\n- Identity rules: after pairing, the profile pins `runnerInstanceId`. If\n  `baseUrl` later resolves to a different instance id → connection blocked\n  with an explicit mismatch warning (re-pair to accept). Editing `baseUrl`\n  clears the token. Two profiles resolving to the same instance id are flagged\n  as duplicates. Authorization headers are never forwarded across cross-origin\n  redirects (fetch wrapper uses `redirect: \"manual\"` for bearer requests).\n- API compatibility: interface publishes the `apiRevision` range it supports;\n  out-of-range runners are blocked with an explicit message (not a soft\n  warning); equal-revision, different-version runners just show an info badge.\n- **RunnerRegistry** (persisted): profiles + `activeRunnerId`, versioned\n  schema (`schemaVersion`) with forward-migration and corrupt-store recovery\n  (reset to same-origin default + toast, never a crash). Storage:\n  `localStorage` on web/PWA (per-origin; the implicit same-origin runner is\n  always present as a non-deletable profile); on Electron, profiles live in\n  the **main process** store with tokens encrypted via `safeStorage`.\n- **Electron security boundary**: remote HTTP/SSE happens in the **main\n  process** (mirroring the proven VSCode extension-host bridge pattern,\n  `src/vscode-extension/bridge.ts`); the renderer gets an IPC-backed\n  `RuntimeAPIs` implementation and never sees bearer tokens. IPC surface is\n  validated per-channel; external links open in the OS browser; navigation is\n  locked to the bundled SPA.\n- **Per-runner connection scope.** Everything currently keyed to \"the server\"\n  becomes owned by a `RunnerConnection` created per profile: `RuntimeAPIs`\n  instance, `LiveEventConnectionManager` (EventSource + cursor),\n  `EventStreamStateManager`, snapshot cache, `WorkerEntriesManager`,\n  notification manager. React context provides the *active* connection;\n  TanStack Query keys gain a `runnerId` prefix (the client profile id).\n  Switching runners swaps the context; inactive connections are **closed** in\n  v1, except the switcher's status ping: `GET /api/healthz` per saved profile,\n  15 s timeout, exponential backoff capped at 5 min, only while the switcher\n  UI is open (no permanent background polling).\n- **State invariants** (per `client-server-state-invariants`):\n  - Ownership: all domain state belongs to the runner; the client persists\n    only runner profiles, UI preferences, and drafts. Drafts are keyed by\n    (runnerId, conversationId) so switching runners never leaks a draft.\n  - Provenance: every snapshot/event frame is tagged with `runnerId` at the\n    connection layer; reducers reject frames whose scope doesn't match.\n  - Freshness: on switch/reconnect, the existing snapshot + `Last-Event-ID`\n    resume protocol runs per runner; `stream.resync_required` unchanged.\n  - Mutations carry their `runnerId`. A mutation completing after a switch is\n    **not** treated as cancelled: its cache updates apply to the originating\n    runner's query keys (which are simply not on screen), and failures surface\n    as a notification naming the runner. Run/conversation ids are only unique\n    per runner; all URL deep links (`/session/:runId`) resolve against the\n    active runner only.\n  - Races to test: rapid switching mid-stream; mutation resolving after\n    switch; token revoked mid-session (401 → profile marked needs-reauth,\n    banner + re-pair CTA); runner restarted mid-conversation (resume via\n    cursor); instance-id mismatch on reconnect.\n\n### 4.6 The 29 direct-fetch call sites\n\nAll migrate to methods on the per-runner `RuntimeAPIs` (extending\n`src/runtime-api/types.ts` with the missing domains: runs, git, fs, attachments,\nprojects/memory, planning, accounts, notifications, terminals). Mechanical but\nwide; `useHomeMutations.ts` (1,269 lines) is split by domain as part of the\nmigration (conversations / runs / planning / settings) to stay under the\n1200-line rule. `InteractiveTerminal.tsx`'s `EventSource` goes through the\nconnection's ticket-aware stream opener.\n\n### 4.7 Filesystem semantics\n\nPaths shown in the UI (project pickers, file viewer, attachments) already flow\nthrough runner APIs (`/api/fs`, `/api/fs/files`, `/api/attachments`) — they are\n**runner paths** and keep working remotely. Co-location is a *capability, not a\nheuristic*: the Electron shell marks a profile co-located only when it launched\nthat runner process itself (or the user explicitly asserts it). Only co-located\nprofiles offer `native.chooseFolder()` and OS editor links\n(`project-file-links.ts`); everything else uses the `/api/fs` picker dialog and\nthe in-app file viewer. \"localhost\" in a URL proves nothing (SSH tunnels) and\nis not used as a signal.\n\n### 4.8 Notifications\n\n- Web/PWA: web push keeps working for the runner that serves the PWA (its\n  origin's service worker + its VAPID keys — the existing flow, unchanged).\n  **Constraint acknowledged**: one SW registration holds one push\n  subscription tied to one VAPID key, so a PWA installed from runner A cannot\n  also receive native push from runner B in v1. Remote runners' events surface\n  in-app while connected; cross-runner push relay is future work.\n- Electron: `RuntimeAPIs.native.notify` already exists; the main process\n  subscribes to the active runner's named events for notification-worthy\n  kinds. Inactive-runner notifications are out of scope for v1.\n\n## 5. User stories (v1 acceptance)\n\n1. **Lean local dev** — As the builder, I run `pnpm dev` and get runner + Vite;\n   combined steady-state RSS < 2 GB under the scripted workload (§4.1); the web\n   UI at the runner port behaves identically to the current app. *(Acceptance:\n   full existing e2e suite passes against the runner-served SPA.)*\n2. **Headless runner** — I run `pnpm runner` on a box with no display; it\n   serves API, SSE, and the built UI on one port (or API-only without\n   `dist/interface`); `omni` CLI and `tests/lifecycle` drive it end-to-end.\n3. **Phone** — I open the runner URL on my phone (tailscale/tunnel), install\n   the PWA, pair via QR, and supervise agents; terminal, composer, and this\n   runner's push notifications work; offline launch shows an explicit\n   runner-unreachable shell, not a stale session.\n4. **Electron remote** — In the desktop app I add a second runner by URL +\n   pairing token, see both in the switcher with status dots, switch between\n   them; each shows its own runs/conversations with correct resume after\n   reconnect; tokens never reach the renderer.\n5. **Failure honesty** — Unreachable runner → explicit offline banner with URL\n   and error detail; reconnect resumes via `Last-Event-ID`; 401 → named runner\n   + re-pair CTA; instance-id mismatch → blocked with explanation.\n6. **Return visit** — Runner profiles, active selection, and UI preferences\n   survive restart on web, PWA, and Electron; a corrupted profile store resets\n   safely to the same-origin default with a visible notice.\n\nSwitcher status states: connected / connecting / offline / needs-reauth /\nincompatible (apiRevision) / identity-mismatch.\n\nOnboarding: Electron first launch with no reachable local runner shows a\n\"Connect a runner\" screen (URL + token / QR paste) instead of an error. Web/PWA\nnever needs onboarding (served by its runner).\n\n## 6. Error transparency & instrumentation\n\n- Existing posture (full error details + stack traces to the frontend) is\n  preserved; `update_error` SSE frames and `app-errors.ts` flows unchanged —\n  with the §4.3.6 redaction rules applied to credentials/tickets.\n- New named events: `runner.bridge_ready`, `runner.bridge_unavailable`,\n  `runner.static_ui_enabled`, `runner.static_ui_missing`,\n  `auth.bearer_issued`, `auth.bearer_origin_rejected`,\n  `auth.stream_ticket_issued`, `auth.unprotected_non_loopback`, plus existing\n  `runtime.started/stopped`.\n- Client-side connection lifecycle logged through `LiveEventConnectionManager`\n  diagnostics; switcher surfaces last error per profile verbatim.\n- Control plane: everything remains scriptable headless (`tests/lifecycle`\n  already proves this). `omni` CLI gains `--runner <url>` / token flags.\n\n## 7. Testing strategy\n\n- **Unit/integration (vitest)**: streaming server (SSE via `startOmniServer`:\n  heartbeat, `Last-Event-ID` resume without duplicates, client-disconnect\n  cleanup, backpressure, flush-per-frame through a compressing proxy); bearer\n  auth (origin binding, wrong-origin rejection, preflight, redirect\n  non-forwarding); stream tickets (single-use atomicity, expiry, concurrent\n  redemption, reconnect-with-fresh-ticket); static serving (bootstrap JSON\n  escaping incl. `</script>`/U+2028, SPA-fallback scope, 404s for `/api/*`,\n  traversal rejection, cache headers); RunnerRegistry (schema migration,\n  corruption recovery, identity pinning/mismatch, baseUrl-edit token clearing);\n  connection races (§4.5 list); network exposure policy (refuse-to-start\n  matrix).\n- **Route parity gate before deleting Next adapters**: table-driven test\n  hitting every registry route through both the Next adapter and\n  `startOmniServer`, comparing status, headers (incl. cookies), and body\n  semantics; includes multipart upload and both SSE endpoints.\n- **Lifecycle suite** (`tests/lifecycle`) runs against `scripts/runner.ts` —\n  the primary backend regression gate.\n- **Playwright e2e**: existing suites repointed at the runner-served SPA; new\n  two-runner spec (two runner processes, separate DBs/data dirs/ports/\n  identities): add → pair → switch → restart one runner → resume. Browser\n  cross-origin spec: SPA from runner A pairs and talks to runner B.\n- **Electron/VSCode**: `tests/electron` updated for the IPC RuntimeAPIs and\n  main-process networking; `tests/vscode` updated for the new default port +\n  bearer wiring (\"behavior unchanged\" still requires these test updates).\n- **Candidate agentic user-journey tests** (require approval before running):\n  (a) phone-viewport PWA pair + send message against a fresh runner;\n  (b) Electron connects to a remote runner, switches, and recovers from a\n  runner restart mid-conversation.\n\n## 8. Milestones\n\nResequenced so multi-runner ships dark until every request path is scoped:\n\n**M1 — Runner stands alone** *(backend only; Next still serves the UI)*\nStreaming fix; `scripts/runner.ts` with boot duties, lifecycle contract,\n`healthz`; `next/server` out of auth/api-errors; route-parity test rig;\nlifecycle suite green against the runner.\n\n**M2 — Interface on Vite, Next deleted**\n`apps/interface`; layout ported; Next-isms removed; static-serving contract +\nbootstrap injection; SW/PWA caching contract; `pnpm dev` = runner + Vite;\nroute-parity + Playwright green; then delete `src/app/api/*`, page/layout/\ninstrumentation, `next.config.ts`, Next deps; `src/app/home/` →\n`src/interface/home/`; memory acceptance measured.\n\n**M3 — Transport unification** *(no user-visible change)*\nShared types to `src/shared/` + ESLint boundary + split typecheck projects;\nall 29 call sites through `RuntimeAPIs`; `useHomeMutations.ts` split by domain;\nquery keys gain `runnerId` prefix (still always `\"local\"`).\n\n**M4 — Remote auth protocol** *(dark: no switcher UI yet)*\nBearer sessions with origin binding; stream tickets + reconnect ownership in\n`LiveEventConnectionManager`; preflight handling; runner identity\n(`instanceId`, `apiRevision`) in bootstrap; network-exposure policy +\nredaction; `omni --runner` flags.\n\n**M5 — Multi-runner UI**\nRunnerRegistry persistence (+ migration/recovery); `RunnerConnection` scoping\nand race handling; switcher + status states; onboarding screen; drafts keyed\nper runner; two-runner Playwright + cross-origin browser specs pass.\n\n**M6 — Electron remote client**\nMain-process networking + IPC RuntimeAPIs; `safeStorage` tokens; co-location\ncapability gating for native pickers/editor links; `tests/electron` updated;\nVSCode port/auth wiring updated.\n\nEach milestone leaves the app fully working; M1–M3 are refactors guarded by\nparity tests, M4 ships dark, M5 turns the feature on, M6 completes the shells.\n\n## 9. Risks & trust surfaces\n\n- **Exposing runners on a network** is the point of this work. Mitigations:\n  refuse-to-start policy (§4.3.5, needs user sign-off), origin-bound bearer\n  sessions, single-use path-bound stream tickets, tokens encrypted at rest in\n  Electron's main process only, never in URLs or logs, rate-limited issuance.\n  Docs recommend tailscale/tunnels over raw port-forwarding.\n- **SSE through proxies/tunnels**: heartbeats exist; streaming rewrite must\n  flush per-frame (tested through a compressing proxy — the scenario that\n  motivated `dev-compression-proxy.ts`).\n- **Single-process API server** remains a hard constraint (in-memory event\n  bus/PTYs). Documented; horizontal scaling is a non-goal.\n- **Churn risk**: M3 touches 29 files. Mitigated by per-domain mechanical PRs,\n  parity tests, and shipping dark until M5.\n- **Electron regression risk**: startup contract changes; covered by\n  `tests/electron` in M6.\n\n## 10. North star & future direction (context, not commitment)\n\nOne interface, installed anywhere, showing all your runners: merged\ncross-runner dashboard, cross-runner push relay, background notifications from\ninactive runners, runner health/resource panel, Tauri/mobile-native shells,\npackage extraction (Approach B) once boundaries settle, runner-to-runner work\nhandoff.\n\n## 11. Open questions for review\n\n1. **Network exposure policy** (§4.3.5): OK to refuse non-loopback start\n   without auth (override via env)? This is stricter than today.\n2. Naming: \"runner\" = API server + bridge pair; docs-level rename only.\n3. Compression proxy (3035) retired; VSCode default port changes — confirm no\n   other consumers of 3035.\n4. Memory acceptance thresholds (< 2 GB dev, < 1 GB prod runner) — adjust?\n"
}

{"type":"item.completed","item":{"id":"item_0","type":"agent_message","text":"## 1. Verdict: HARDEN\n\nThis revision resolves the original ten findings well. The design is close to executable, but a few remaining issues could break first-run setup, weaken origin binding, or leave Electron/VSCode unusable between milestones.\n\n## 2. Highest-impact findings\n\n### 1. A fresh headless runner has no secure first-authentication path\n\nThe runner refuses a non-loopback bind when authentication is not configured, but the phone story assumes the user can open that runner and pair. On a fresh headless machine, it is unclear how the first password or pairing token is created without already having authenticated UI access.\n\nConcrete fix:\n\n- Add an explicit provisioning workflow such as `omni auth init`.\n- Generate either a password hash or a short-lived, single-use bootstrap pairing token.\n- Print secrets only to an attached TTY or write them to a user-selected file with restrictive permissions; fail in unattended mode unless a secret source is supplied.\n- Support an environment variable or `--password-file`/`--pair-token-file` for automated deployments.\n- Define whether the existing QR flow requires an authenticated session and how a headless user obtains an equivalent URL/token via CLI.\n- Add an acceptance test starting with an empty database: provision auth, bind non-loopback, pair a phone-origin interface, then invalidate the bootstrap token.\n\nWithout this, the stricter network policy can create a setup dead end.\n\n### 2. Origin binding still has an originless-request bypass unless enforced from stored session provenance\n\nThe wording says non-browser clients without an `Origin` header are exempt. If that exemption is decided per request, a browser-issued token stolen from storage can be used by `curl` simply by omitting `Origin`.\n\nConcrete fix:\n\nAdd at least these fields to the session model:\n\n```ts\ntransport: \"cookie\" | \"bearer\";\nboundOrigin: string | null;\nclientKind: \"browser\" | \"native\";\n```\n\nThen enforce:\n\n- A browser bearer session with `boundOrigin` always requires that exact origin. Missing, different, or `Origin: null` is rejected.\n- Only a session originally issued through an originless native flow may have `boundOrigin: null`.\n- Cookie session tokens are never accepted as bearer tokens, and bearer sessions are never installed as cookies.\n- Stream-ticket redemption validates the request origin against the session’s stored origin, not merely the ticket.\n- CORS headers are applied to matching-origin error responses as well as successful responses so the interface can see real API errors.\n- `/api/healthz` explicitly allows non-credentialed cross-origin GETs; otherwise the browser’s switcher status checks will fail.\n\nAlso clarify that long-lived session tokens never appear in URLs. The existing QR/deep-link pairing token does appear in a URL; it should be short-lived, single-use, removed immediately with `history.replaceState`, covered by `Referrer-Policy: no-referrer`, and included in proxy/log redaction guidance.\n\n### 3. Removing Next in M2 may break Electron before its M6 conversion\n\nThe spec says Electron becomes a Vite-based remote client in M6, but Next is removed in M2. If Electron currently loads or packages the Next application, it may be broken for four milestones. Similarly, VSCode’s port change cannot wait until M6 if port 3035 disappears in M2.\n\nConcrete fix:\n\n- In M2, make Electron load the Vite production bundle while preserving its current local runtime transport.\n- Move the VSCode default-port change and its regression test to M2.\n- Move VSCode bearer support to M4 when bearer authentication appears.\n- In M5, enable multi-runner only on web/PWA; hide or disable its controls in Electron until M6.\n- In M6, replace Electron’s preserved local transport with main-process remote networking and enable the switcher there.\n\nState this transitional shell contract explicitly so “each milestone leaves the app working” is testable.\n\n### 4. The development URL still contradicts the acceptance story\n\n`pnpm dev` runs Vite with `/api` proxied to the runner, which normally means the live UI is at Vite’s port. The acceptance story still says the UI at the runner port behaves identically. Unless the runner reverse-proxies Vite, the runner port will serve a stale production bundle, an API-only page, or nothing.\n\nChoose one:\n\n- Recommended: development UI is at `http://localhost:5173`; production and e2e packaged-mode UI are at the runner port.\n- Alternatively, make the runner reverse-proxy non-API development traffic to Vite so port 3050 remains canonical.\n\nUpdate the memory workload and Playwright targets to distinguish Vite development mode from runner-served production mode.\n\n### 5. HTTPS enforcement needs a trusted-proxy contract\n\nA runner behind an HTTPS reverse proxy commonly sees an internal plain HTTP connection. The current rule could reject valid bearer issuance, while blindly trusting `X-Forwarded-Proto` would let clients spoof HTTPS.\n\nConcrete fix:\n\n- Add an explicit trusted-proxy setting containing allowed proxy addresses or networks.\n- Only honor `Forwarded`/`X-Forwarded-Proto` when the immediate peer is trusted.\n- Otherwise determine security from the actual socket.\n- Document the supported Tailscale/reverse-proxy setup.\n- Test direct HTTP, direct HTTPS, trusted TLS termination, spoofed forwarded headers, and multiple forwarded values.\n\nAll requests containing pairing tokens or bearer credentials—including initial pairing—should use `redirect: \"manual\"` so secrets cannot follow redirects.\n\n### 6. The shutdown SSE frame has the wrong meaning\n\n`stream.resync_required` means the client’s cursor can no longer be trusted. Using it as a shutdown goodbye can trigger a snapshot reload precisely while the runner is stopping.\n\nConcrete fix:\n\n- Add a distinct `runner.stopping` SSE event or close-reason frame.\n- Stop listening for new connections.\n- Send the stopping event to existing streams.\n- Allow a short bounded flush period, then abort remaining readers.\n- Give PTYs and child processes explicit graceful and forced-shutdown deadlines.\n- State whether the bridge is terminated or merely released when it was adopted rather than spawned by this runner.\n\n“Report the owning PID” on an API port conflict should be best-effort unless the runner also owns a reliable lockfile; cross-platform bind errors do not inherently provide the other process’s PID.\n\n### 7. Browser token storage should be paired with a frontend security policy\n\nOrigin binding substantially reduces token portability, but same-origin injected code can still perform authenticated actions directly. Because browser tokens live in `localStorage`, the interface needs an explicit script and navigation policy.\n\nConcrete fix:\n\n- Define a CSP with `script-src 'self'` and hashes/nonces for the theme bootstrap.\n- Disallow third-party scripts and uncontrolled plugin code in the interface origin.\n- Restrict framing with `frame-ancestors`.\n- Define a workable `connect-src` policy for user-added HTTPS runners and loopback development.\n- Sanitize any server-provided content rendered as HTML.\n- Add a test ensuring bootstrap content, runner errors, project paths, and agent output cannot introduce executable markup.\n\nThis does not require changing storage technology; it makes the accepted browser-storage risk explicit.\n\n### 8. Tighten two verification details\n\nThe route-parity and memory gates need small clarifications:\n\n- SSE parity cannot compare complete response bodies because streams do not finish. Compare status/headers and a bounded sequence of normalized frames, then abort and verify cleanup.\n- Stateful routes should run against isolated fixtures rather than invoking both adapters sequentially against the same database.\n- Define memory as the total process tree, including the API process, bridge, gateway children, Vite, and watchers as applicable.\n- Treat memory thresholds as a controlled benchmark gate unless CI hardware is fixed; record peak and end-of-workload RSS, not only a single steady-state sample.\n\n## 3. What I would leave alone\n\nI would retain the revised choices around:\n\n- Origin-bound browser bearer sessions and open CORS only for authentication issuance.\n- Manager-owned EventSource reconnection with fresh, path-bound tickets and explicit cursors.\n- Electron main-process networking with an IPC transport.\n- Durable server identity, profile pinning, manual redirect handling, and API revision blocking.\n- The offline static shell and refusal to cache personalized `index.html`.\n- Refusing insecure network startup, subject to confirming the provisioning workflow and user sign-off.\n- Running the production runner through `tsx`; the important requirement is that no frontend compiler, watcher, or Next process runs in production.\n- API-only mode when no interface artifact is installed.\n- The six-stage sequencing, after fixing the Electron/VSCode transitional contracts.\n- One active runner, scoped drafts/caches/mutations, and no inactive-runner streams.\n- Limiting web push to the PWA’s hosting runner in v1.\n- In-place repository boundaries rather than immediate package extraction.\n- The two-process runner topology and single-process API constraint.\n- Full error transparency with mandatory secret redaction.\n\nOnce first-run authentication, stored-origin enforcement, intermediate shell compatibility, and the development URL are specified, I would consider the design READY."}}

## Claude thinking · 2026-07-29T12:05:47.873Z

I'm doing a second pass to harden the spec with eight findings, and while it's close to executable, there are four specific gaps I need to address: first-run auth provisioning, stored-origin enforcement, intermediate shell compatibility across Electron and VSCode versions, and the dev URL story. I'll also need to tackle proxy trust, shutdown frame handling, CSP, and test details. Rather than rewriting everything, I'm applying targeted edits—starting with adding a provisioning section that documents the `omni auth init` command and password handling.

For origin binding, I'm defining session model fields to track transport, bound origin, and client kind, ensuring browser sessions require exact origin matching while native-issued sessions can be null, and cookie tokens are never accepted as bearer tokens. Stream tickets validate origin from the session, CORS headers appear on error responses, and the healthz endpoint allows non-credentialed cross-origin GET requests. Pair tokens in URLs are short-lived and single-use with history.replaceState and Referrer-Policy set to no-referrer.

The milestone transitional contract spans M2 through M6: Electron loads the Vite bundle with current local transport in M2, VSCode port changes and gets tested in M2, bearer token support lands in M4, M5 moves to web/PWA only with Electron hidden, and M6 adds Electron remote with a switcher. For the dev URL story, the dev UI runs at the Vite port while the runner port is canonical for production and e2e packaged mode, and I need to update the acceptance story accordingly.

Trusted proxy handling requires checking OMNIHARNESS_TRUSTED_PROXIES and only honoring X-Forwarded-Proto from trusted peers, with manual redirects for all credentialed requests including pairing. Shutdown involves a distinct runner.stopping SSE frame with bounded flush, PTY deadlines, handling both adopted and spawned bridges differently, and best-effort port-conflict PID detection. CSP for the interface uses script-src 'self' plus a hash for the theme script, frame-ancestors none, and a connect-src policy that needs user-added runner origins while allowing https and loopback, though there's tension between these requirements—I also need to sanitize rendered content and test this. For verification, SSE parity means matching status and headers with bounded normalized frames in isolated fixtures, memory tracking captures the process tree with peak and end values recorded, and the benchmark gate shouldn't depend on CI hardware.

  "old_string": "5. **Network exposure policy** (changes current permissive behavior —\n   flagged for user confirmation): binding the API server to a non-loopback\n   host with no auth configured **refuses to start** unless\n   `OMNIHARNESS_ALLOW_UNAUTHENTICATED_NETWORK=1` is set; bearer issuance over\n   plaintext non-loopback HTTP is likewise refused without the same explicit\n   override. Loopback HTTP stays frictionless. `auth.unprotected_non_loopback`\n   named event is emitted whenever the override is used.\n6. **Redaction**: `Authorization` headers, pair tokens, stream tickets, and\n   token-bearing URLs are scrubbed from logs, error payloads, and named-event\n   details.\n7. **Next removal**: `NextRequest` usage in `auth/guards.ts`,\n   `auth/session.ts`, `api-errors.ts` is replaced with plain Fetch `Request`\n   (the 9-line shim `routes/next-request.ts` shows this is mechanical).",
  "new_string": "5. **Network exposure policy** (changes current permissive behavior —\n   flagged for user confirmation): binding the API server to a non-loopback\n   host with no auth configured **refuses to start** unless\n   `OMNIHARNESS_ALLOW_UNAUTHENTICATED_NETWORK=1` is set; bearer issuance over\n   plaintext non-loopback HTTP is likewise refused without the same explicit\n   override. Loopback HTTP stays frictionless. `auth.unprotected_non_loopback`\n   named event is emitted whenever the override is used.\n6. **Trusted-proxy contract** (HTTPS behind reverse proxies / tunnels): the\n   runner honors `Forwarded`/`X-Forwarded-Proto` only when the immediate\n   socket peer is in `OMNIHARNESS_TRUSTED_PROXIES` (addresses/CIDRs);\n   otherwise protocol security is judged from the actual socket. Prevents\n   spoofed `X-Forwarded-Proto: https` from downgrading the plaintext rule\n   above. Docs cover the supported tailscale / TLS-terminating-proxy setups.\n   All credentialed client requests — including initial pairing — use\n   `redirect: \"manual\"` so secrets never follow redirects.\n7. **First-run provisioning** (headless box, fresh DB — required so the\n   refuse-to-start policy has no dead end): `omni auth init` generates either\n   a password (stored as argon2 hash) or a **single-use, short-lived bootstrap\n   pairing token**, and prints the pairing URL/QR to the attached TTY — or\n   writes it to a file with `0600` perms via `--pair-token-file` for\n   unattended deploys. In unattended mode with no secret source configured,\n   the runner fails with instructions rather than starting open. The normal QR\n   flow (which requires an authenticated session) is unchanged; `omni auth\n   pair-url` prints an equivalent pairing link for an already-provisioned\n   headless runner. Acceptance test: empty DB → provision → non-loopback bind\n   → pair a phone-origin interface → bootstrap token invalidated after use.\n8. **Redaction**: `Authorization` headers, pair tokens, stream tickets, and\n   token-bearing URLs are scrubbed from logs, error payloads, and named-event\n   details.\n9. **Next removal**: `NextRequest` usage in `auth/guards.ts`,\n   `auth/session.ts`, `api-errors.ts` is replaced with plain Fetch `Request`\n   (the 9-line shim `routes/next-request.ts` shows this is mechanical)."
}

}

  "new_string": "**M2 — Interface on Vite, Next deleted**\n`apps/interface`; layout ported; Next-isms removed; static-serving contract +\nbootstrap injection; SW/PWA caching contract; `pnpm dev` = runner + Vite;\nroute-parity + Playwright green; then delete `src/app/api/*`, page/layout/\ninstrumentation, `next.config.ts`, Next deps; `src/app/home/` →\n`src/interface/home/`; memory acceptance measured.\n*Transitional shell contract*: Electron switches to loading the Vite\nproduction bundle in this milestone while keeping its current local runtime\ntransport (it must not break for four milestones); VSCode's default port\nmoves off the retired 3035 here too, with its regression test.\n\n**M3 — Transport unification** *(no user-visible change)*\nShared types to `src/shared/` + ESLint boundary + split typecheck projects;\nall 29 call sites through `RuntimeAPIs`; `useHomeMutations.ts` split by domain;\nquery keys gain `runnerId` prefix (still always `\"local\"`).\n\n**M4 — Remote auth protocol** *(dark: no switcher UI yet)*\nBearer sessions with stored-provenance origin binding; stream tickets +\nreconnect ownership in `LiveEventConnectionManager`; preflight handling;\ntrusted-proxy contract; first-run provisioning (`omni auth init`); runner\nidentity (`instanceId`, `apiRevision`) in bootstrap; network-exposure policy +\nredaction; `omni --runner` flags; VSCode gains bearer wiring here.\n\n**M5 — Multi-runner UI (web/PWA only)**\nRunnerRegistry persistence (+ migration/recovery); `RunnerConnection` scoping\nand race handling; switcher + status states; onboarding screen; drafts keyed\nper runner; two-runner Playwright + cross-origin browser specs pass. Switcher\ncontrols stay hidden in Electron until M6.\n\n**M6 — Electron remote client**\nMain-process networking + IPC RuntimeAPIs; `safeStorage` tokens; co-location\ncapability gating for native pickers/editor links; switcher enabled in\nElectron; `tests/electron` updated.\n\nEach milestone leaves every shell working; M1–M3 are refactors guarded by\nparity tests, M4 ships dark, M5 turns multi-runner on for web/PWA, M6\ncompletes Electron."

{"type":"item.completed","item":{"id":"item_0","type":"agent_message","text":"## 1. Verdict: READY\n\nThe spec is ready to drive implementation. The remaining findings are execution details that can be added to milestone acceptance criteria; none requires another architectural redesign.\n\n## 2. Final findings\n\n### 1. Correct the security claim about origin-bound tokens\n\nStored origin provenance prevents a bearer token from being used by JavaScript running on a different browser origin. It does not stop a stolen token from being replayed by `curl`, because a non-browser client can forge the expected `Origin` header.\n\nConcrete fix:\n\n- Describe origin binding as browser-origin isolation, not cryptographic token binding.\n- Continue requiring the stored origin exactly as designed.\n- State that bearer tokens remain replayable secrets; theft resistance such as DPoP or device keys is out of scope.\n- Retain CSP, short session lifetimes, revocation, redaction, and encrypted Electron storage as the v1 protections.\n\nThe implementation remains valid; only the threat-model claim needs correction.\n\n### 2. Let the cached PWA work when its hosting runner is down\n\nThe offline shell currently calls its same-origin bootstrap and shows “runner unreachable.” It should not let that failure block access to other saved runners.\n\nConcrete fix:\n\n- Mount the application and load `RunnerRegistry` before or independently of same-origin bootstrap success.\n- Mark the implicit same-origin runner offline when bootstrap fails.\n- If the saved active runner is remote, attempt that runner normally.\n- Keep the switcher usable so the user can move from unavailable Runner A to reachable Runner B.\n- Add a test: install from A, save B, stop A, launch from the service-worker cache, connect to B.\n\nThis better fulfills the “self-contained interface” goal.\n\n### 3. Define identity behavior when a runner database is copied\n\nBecause `runnerInstanceId` lives in the settings table, cloning or restoring the database may create two live runners with the same identity.\n\nConcrete fix—choose one documented rule:\n\n- Prefer storing instance identity in machine/deployment-local metadata outside the transferable domain database; or\n- Treat identity as belonging to the copied dataset and provide `omni runner rekey` for clones.\n\nTest backup/restore and clone behavior. The client should never silently treat two simultaneously reachable endpoints with one identity as interchangeable.\n\n### 4. Add secure fallback behavior for native secret storage and CLI tokens\n\nElectron `safeStorage` is not guaranteed to provide strong encryption on every platform, particularly when no system key store is available.\n\nConcrete fix:\n\n- Check whether encryption is available.\n- If unavailable, default to session-only tokens or ask for explicit permission before plaintext persistence.\n- Surface the degraded state instead of claiming tokens are encrypted.\n\nFor the CLI, avoid session tokens in command-line arguments because they can appear in shell history and process listings. Prefer environment variables, stdin, keychain storage, or `--token-file`; reserve a direct `--token` option for an explicitly warned compatibility path.\n\n### 5. Make three small items explicit in milestone gates\n\n- Existing `auth_sessions` rows must migrate to `transport: \"cookie\"` without invalidating current same-origin sessions.\n- Serve CSP as an HTTP header—`frame-ancestors` is not reliably enforceable through a meta tag—and preserve the policy on the cached offline response. Add the needed `default-src`, `style-src`, `font-src`, `img-src`, and `worker-src` directives.\n- Make the open decisions actual checkpoints: audit all consumers of port 3035 before M2, obtain network-policy sign-off before M4, and calibrate memory limits before treating them as benchmark failures.\n\n## 3. What I would leave alone\n\nI would keep the rest of the design as written, especially:\n\n- The in-place two-target repository structure.\n- Removing Next after the route-parity and packaged-UI gates.\n- The transitional Electron and VSCode milestones.\n- Origin-aware browser sessions and separate native sessions.\n- Path-bound, single-use stream tickets with manager-owned reconnection.\n- Trusted-proxy enforcement and manual redirect handling.\n- Refuse-to-start network security with an explicit insecure override.\n- Headless first-run provisioning.\n- Server-generated identity pinning and API revision blocking.\n- Main-process Electron networking and IPC-backed APIs.\n- The static offline shell and personalized-index caching prohibition.\n- Per-runner managers, query keys, drafts, mutations, and cursor ownership.\n- Web/PWA-first multi-runner rollout before enabling Electron.\n- API-only runner deployments.\n- The two-process topology and single-process API constraint.\n- The six milestones and the dark launch through M4.\n- Deferring cross-runner push, inactive connections, merged dashboards, Tauri, and monorepo extraction.\n\nThis is now specific enough for engineers to implement without inventing missing architecture along the way."}}

## Claude thinking · 2026-07-29T12:10:20.775Z

I've got five small corrections to work through before presenting this to the user. First, I need to clarify the threat model language in section 4.3.1—origin binding provides browser-origin isolation rather than replay protection, since curl can still forge the Origin header. Second, I should verify that the offline PWA setup allows reaching other runners independently, meaning the app mounts and registry load without relying on same-origin bootstrap, and I'll test the scenario where one runner is down but others remain accessible.

For the runnerInstanceId storage, I'm going with the simplest approach: keep the identity tied to the dataset and use `omni runner rekey` to manage it. I also need to add fallback handling for safeStorage availability and tighten CLI token hygiene by supporting `--token-file` and environment variables instead of passing tokens through command arguments. Finally, I'm documenting the smaller decisions as checkpoints: auth_sessions migration defaults to cookie transport, CSP gets specified as an HTTP header with full directives including offline cached responses, and I'm flagging three open items for audit gates—the 3035 audit before M2, network policy sign-off before M4, and memory calibration work.

## Claude · 2026-07-29T12:11:25.221Z

The design is done and reviewer-approved. Spec written to `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-29-interface-runner-split-design.md`.

**The headline finding**: this split is already ~70% architected in your codebase. All 62 API routes go through a framework-free registry (`src/runtime/http/`), a standalone Node server exists, the client transport abstraction (`src/runtime-api/`) already takes a `baseUrl`, and a non-Next SPA mount exists. Next.js is doing almost nothing: the app tree is `ssr: false`, so "SSR" is just a boot skeleton plus an inlined bootstrap JSON blob — which the runner can inject into `index.html` itself.

**The design in one paragraph**: the **runner** becomes a headless Node service (`scripts/runner.ts`) — DB, supervisor, bridge (still a separate process per your choice), terminals, SSE, auth — serving the API and the built Vite SPA on one port. The **interface** becomes a static Vite app: same web URL, same PWA phone install, plus the existing Electron shell converted to a pure remote client. Multi-runner works via saved runner profiles with a switcher; runners get a server-generated `instanceId` and remote clients authenticate with origin-bound bearer tokens (cookies stay for the same-origin web path).

**The real work, sequenced in 6 milestones** (each leaves everything working): M1 fix the standalone server's SSE streaming + runner entrypoint; M2 Vite SPA, delete Next (this kills the 8 GB dev process — dev becomes `tsx watch` + Vite); M3 migrate the 29 hardcoded `fetch('/api/...')` call sites through `RuntimeAPIs`; M4 remote auth protocol (ships dark); M5 multi-runner switcher on web/PWA; M6 Electron remote client.

**Review process**: Codex reviewed it three times in one session — HARDEN → HARDEN → **READY**. It caught real design flaws I fixed: default-closed CORS would have broken the phone-connects-to-runner-B story (now: origin-bound bearer sessions), single-use SSE tickets clashing with EventSource auto-reconnect (now: manager-owned reconnection), Electron tokens reaching the renderer (now: main-process networking over IPC), and a headless first-run dead end (now: `omni auth init`). Its final pass-3 nits are folded in.

**Three things need your sign-off in the spec** (§11): refusing non-loopback start without auth configured (stricter than today, env override exists); retiring the 3035 compression proxy; and the memory acceptance targets (< 2 GB dev, < 1 GB prod runner).

Please review the spec and let me know if you want changes before we move to the implementation plan — also tell me then whether you want the plan executed in the repo directly or in worktrees.

uuid: 4a9a70c6-d61d-4dfa-a7bd-47a9aa6b54a6
parent: d517bdd3-2d47-4a58-a6c2-980c2d6fdf09
